Phase 0 (Foundation) was executed on 2026-10-10 from `2026-10-09-phase-0-foundation.md`. Every task passed its review and the final whole-branch review found nothing Critical. This file lists what was left open on purpose, grouped by when to deal with it. Read the relevant section when writing each later phase's plan, and delete items as they are done.

## Phase 7 (OCR)

The research write-up (`docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-14-phase-7-ocr-research.md`, 2026-10-14) supersedes the spec's section 6.5 where they differ: PaddleOCR PP-OCRv4 in a child process of low priority through onnxruntime-node, Pine's own layer around the models (no `@gutenye/ocr-node`), the tier from the game's head icon by template matching, the boss from the CP, raid egg pictures from pogo_assets; the harness and samples are in the git-ignored `var/ocr-research/`.

From Matt (2026-10-11), with sample screenshots:

- 21 raid screenshots are in `C:\Users\<you>\Downloads`, named `<phone> <what> Day.<ext>`: three phones ("Android" and "Android B" as `.jpg`, "iPhone" as `.png`); eggs of T1, T3, T5, shadow T5 and mega, and the bosses Elgyem, Yveltal and Shadow Landorus. They are not in the repository: copy them to a git-ignored place to work with them, and ask before any becomes a committed fixture. They were taken in production's `squirrelbreeze` region, which decides the gym search (gyms from a scratch copy of the production dump only, never in tests).
- They are a start, not the whole range: phones differ in aspect ratio, which moves every element that is read, and "Day" is in the names because the colours differ by time of day, most near sunset.
- The time comes from the phone's clock in the screenshot plus the countdown (until the hatch for an egg, until the end for a hatched raid). The clock is top-left on these three phones but top-right on some Androids: look for it anywhere in the status bar. Matt offered to walk through what the old code means to do.
- Some phones show no clock in the screenshot at all: the time is then guessed from when the screenshot was uploaded, as the legacy bot did (and often had to, because it failed to read a clock that was there). Keep that fallback, and say in the post when a time is a guess (the legacy bot had `timeWarn`).
- Do not read the tier with OCR as the legacy bot did: count the tier heads with a better method.
- The legacy code cropped many candidate areas to find each element, and pre-processed the image heavily (black and white only). A current Tesseract.js may read noisy screenshots well enough without that: try the raw image first. Compare other OCR engines too before choosing (PaddleOCR for one), weighing accuracy against what runs in Node on Linux in Docker.
- The gym name is often cut off in a screenshot, which is why a screenshot report takes the top hit of the fuzzy gym search without asking.
- The bar to beat: the legacy reader never got past about 90 to 95 percent for the gym (the part that matters most), and the tier was right perhaps 75 to 80 percent of the time, the time not much better. Measure the new reader against the samples per element.

- **A time zone per server** (Matt, 2026-10-14): the phone's clock on a screenshot is read in `settings.timezone`, the one zone of the installation, as every typed time is. An installation that serves servers in several zones would need a zone per server (a column of `Guild`, set by staff, its help saying what it is for: typed times, the clock of screenshots, dates in posts). Not needed for self-hosted instances; decide when such an installation exists. The reader takes a clock that does not fit the upload time as unread and guesses from the upload, so a wrong zone gives a guessed time, never a wrong one beyond the window.
- **Clocks the reader may miss:** a morning/afternoon word before the time is passed over (two characters, or a word of its own), but clocks in other digits than 0–9 are not read. Expect the odd East Asian screenshot in the review folder.
- **A shadow T3 screenshot for staging** (Matt, 2026-10-14): uploaded to `#test-region` on the test server. Use it in the 7B staging (`/read-screenshot`, a report from a screenshot): it is a shadow Kirlia raid with CP 8554, which confirms the 4,000 stamina plan 7A gives a shadow T3 boss (`bossStamina`; 3,600 would give 8115): the report should decide Kirlia by the CP. Its gym is probably not one Pine knows: expect `no-gym` or `gym-unclear`, not a wrong gym.

## Phase 8 (cutover, first push; the Gantt chart's phase 16 since 2026-10-18)

**Decided with Matt (2026-10-15, planning Phase 8):**

- Order: 8A first (the pass over the look of the posts and the table of command names, with Matt in front of Discord; the short staging of the 7F fix round runs during it), then the cutover tooling, the rehearsal and guides, the cutover itself.
- Legacy parties are **not imported** (this replaces spec 9.1 step 5): raids, trains and meetups active at cutover lapse, members are told in the announcement, and the cleanup deletes the channels of every legacy party, trains and meetups included.
  - **Changed by Matt (2026-10-21), for the cutover work:** ~~not built yet~~ Done 2026-10-22 (8C-K): the list's channels that stay are `stays` (`staysAtCutover`), `cutover-check --legacy-parties` counts them, and without the legacy files an orphan named like a train or meetup is left to a person rather than deleted (it may be an open one). The runbook (R4, R5, N5), the guide (4.9, "What changes") and both announcements say so; the announcements were over 2,000 characters a message and are now split (Pittsburgh's post in two, the release post in three). The decision: `/admin cleanup-legacy` no longer deletes the channels of legacy trains and meetups, nor of any party saved with `!save-party` (`deletionTime` −1 in its file): it lists them under "Not deleted", each with its name, when it was made and how many signed up, for the admin to delete by hand the ones that are dead; the rest stay as plain channels (their old status post stays, nothing answers it). Why: several are long-lived discussion channels (Pittsburgh's 16 trains and 3 meetups still open include "Wayfarer Discussion and Planning", two PokéStop submission threads and tourney signups). Legacy Pine never deletes a train or meetup without an end time (`app/party-manager.js:99`), which is why they survive; none of the 19 is saved with −1. Raid channels and the orphans are deleted as before. The runbook's cleanup steps and the cutover announcement say so (the kept channels stay for discussion; new trains and meetups come from `/train` and `/meetup`).
- Production moves to a Linux host that does not exist yet; Matt installs it when needed (suggested: Debian 13, which `node:24-slim` is built on too).
- Phase 9 (Docker packaging) comes before the cutover: production starts on the container, and the rehearsal with the copy of the production database runs in the same setup.
- No fixed date: the cutover happens as soon as the rehearsal passes (the intents end about 2026-11-11).
- **The whole of Pine is one Docker Compose stack** (Matt, 2026-10-15, likely): the bot and its MySQL in the stack, the stack run as one service on the Debian host. The details of the host (partitioning, the service unit, backups on Linux) are not decided yet.
- 8A: Claude prepares first (the legacy messages beside the new posts, a few layout options, the table of command names), then goes through them with Matt with the bot up.
- **8A answers (Matt, 2026-10-15, on the workbook https://claude.ai/artifact/XQBhoHSHvacUPxA7qQEpWD):**
  - The post takes the legacy layout (Option A): the boss block, the statuses side by side as inline fields per group, the reaction emoji on the buttons together with their words (🤔 Interested, 🏃 Join, ✅ Here, 🎉 Done, 👋 Leave, ⬇️ ⬆️, 📡 Remote).
  - The server's own emoji are used where they exist, found by name as the legacy `Helper.getEmoji` did (among every server the bot is in, lower case): the teams (`instinct`, `mystic`, `valor`, `teamless`), the types and the weather of the weaknesses and the catch CP; where a name has none, words (types, weather) or the Unicode emoji (teams).
  - The reporter stays in the description with the mention, as now.
  - Wording as the legacy bot had it: "Level 5 raid", the statuses "Present" and "Complete" in the post (the buttons and commands keep Here and Done, as the legacy reactions and `!here`/`!done` did).
  - Trains and meetups follow the raid post's new look.
  - **Later the same evening, superseding the embed layout:** the posts become Discord's newer message layout (components v2: a container of text blocks, sections with a button or thumbnail beside them, separators), drafted at https://claude.ai/artifact/KqYyaWjfA5SjeUEozCeb4Q and posted as samples on the test server (`var/stage/v2-samples.mts`); Matt: "These are pretty". Each status line has its button beside it (Interested, Join, Here, Done), with counts in every heading of every group (the +1s counted), no emoji in headings (only on buttons), names not cut; with several groups a heading per group (label, trainers, meeting time) and a "Join <group>" beside its lines; the reporter or maker at the very bottom in small text with the mention; a map link button; one message per post (forum messages cannot be inserted later; five groups fit the 40 components).
  - **Application emoji** (Matt liked it): the in-game type, weather and team icons uploaded to the bot's application, so every installation has them; a server's own emoji by the same name, then words, as fallbacks.
  - Command names: `/maybe` becomes `/interested`, with `/hmm` as a second name (the alias most used); `/coming` is a second name of `/join`; `/little`, `/great`, `/ultra` and `/master` are commands of their own, `/rank` with the league fixed; `/boss` stays without a second name.
  - `/counters` gets the number of trainers Pokebattler estimates for the options chosen (counter level, friendship and so on); `howmany` is not a command of its own.
  - **Gym import is wanted back** (the legacy `import-gyms`): entering every gym by hand would make onboarding a new server a huge pain. Where the gyms come from is the research item of the Wayfarer export (memory: the true gym list from Scopely's Wayfarer site). Not needed for Pittsburgh's cutover; it belongs to the onboarding work, but before other instances are told to move.
- Other instances get a guide only, no patch of the legacy bot. The legacy JS stays as it is on its own branch (`main-dev` as it stands, tagged), and is removed entirely from the new bot's tree, so a built image carries none of it.

- `guildDelete` hard-deletes the Guild row and cascades its regions, roles and notifications (as the legacy bot does). Confirm it is still wanted.
- `ci.yml`: runs twice per PR push; no `timeout-minutes`; no `permissions` block.
- **Production has no privileged intents** (Matt, 2026-10-12). Discord reviews an app for Server Members, Presence and Message Content once the unique users of all its servers reach 10,000 ("What are Privileged Intents?"; 90 days to apply after the notice); Pittsburgh's server alone is above that, and Matt was notified and did not apply: by Discord's e-mail, checked on 2026-10-12, Pittsburgh's application keeps its privileged intents for 30 more days, so they end about **2026-11-11** (other instances of Pine: unknown). The legacy bot asks for Server Members and Presence at login and needs the member list (who gets a notification, `iam`), so from that day it will presumably be refused at login or lose those features: the cutover, an application to Discord for review, or a legacy bot patched to run without those intents has to come before it (Matt decides). For the new bot assume none, and do not plan on gaining approval: `settings.autoAssignRole` (Server Members) and `settings.ocr.passive` (Message Content) cannot work in production, and nothing may need a member list, at cutover either. Matt (2026-10-12): the auto-assigned role is not built (Discord's own onboarding does it better today; plan 4C leaves it out, and the setting, the `GuildMembers` intent flag and the `AutoAssignRole` table stay unused); passive screenshot reading is not needed either (a mention with an image, or `/raid` with an attachment, is enough), so Phase 7 builds those two ways first and the passive one only if it costs nothing.
- **A migration plan for the admins of other Pine instances** (Matt, 2026-10-12). Pittsburgh is expected to cut over well before its intents end, but every instance above 10,000 users gets the same notice from Discord with its own date, and some will not have migrated by then. Write, with the cutover documents (Phase 8, and the packaging of Phase 9 which a new install needs): (1) how to tell whether an instance is affected and when (the developer portal, Discord's e-mail); (2) the migration itself, step by step, for somebody who did not follow this rewrite (database takeover, settings and `.env`, forums, the command registration, what members will notice); (3) for those who are not ready in time, the stopgaps and what each costs: applying to Discord for review, or running the legacy bot without the privileged intents (which lines of the legacy code ask for them and what stops working: notification recipients, `iam` paging, the auto-assigned role). Whether the last is a document only or a patch on the legacy branch is Matt's to decide; the rule against editing legacy JS stands until he does.
- **Unown at cutover** (Matt, 2026-10-12; plan 4B makes Unown an ordinary species whose report is also posted in the sightings channel, and the bot no longer assigns or pings the `unown` role). Holders of the `unown` role should end up with a spawn want for Unown, but a role's holders cannot be listed without the Server Members intent. Decided by Matt (2026-10-12), to build in plan 4B: a member whose interaction shows the `unown` role and who has no Unown want in that server gets a spawn want for Unown, once per member and server (remembered, so that a member who removes the want is not given it again); plus a line in the cutover announcement.

## Phase 9 (packaging; the Gantt chart's phase 10 since 2026-10-18)

**Agreed with the Wayfarer / web map session (Matt, 2026-10-15, evening):** the cutover tooling and Docker run in this checkout beside 8M-1..8M-5 and 8W-2 in the Wayfarer worktree; they barely touch the same code.

- **Public address:** Matt bought `profpine.com` (at Cloudflare); Pine's web pages are reached through a Cloudflare Tunnel (`cloudflared`), so Pine itself only serves HTTP and knows nothing of how the outside reaches it. 8M-1 gives the settings `web.host` (default `127.0.0.1`), `web.port`, `web.publicUrl` (e.g. `https://map.profpine.com`) and `web.trustProxy`; the compose file sets them.
- **Tunnel in Compose:** a `cloudflared` container cannot reach Pine's `127.0.0.1` (every container has its own loopback). Either Pine listens on `0.0.0.0` inside the compose network without publishing the port to the host, or `cloudflared` runs with `network_mode: service:pine`. To pick in the Docker plan; leaning to the first (two ordinary services, restarted independently). The tunnel token is an environment variable, never a file in the repository.
- **Migrations:** 8W-1 has 012, 8B-3 has 013; 014 onwards goes to whoever merges first, the other renumbers at its merge. A Docker plan probably adds none.
- **Shared files** (`CLAUDE.md`, the follow-ups, the Gantt data, the settings, `.env.example`) conflict at every merge; whoever merges second resolves them.

- New installs need MySQL 8+ (collation in the generated DDL).
- `pino-pretty` is a devDependency but is loaded when `NODE_ENV=development`.
- A missing settings file is reported as "not valid JSON: ENOENT…".
- A new install that fails half-way is reported as "not a Professor Pine database" on retry (MySQL DDL is not transactional).
- Signal handlers are registered only after startup completes.
- `.env.example`: say that `GITHUB_TOKEN` and `GITHUB_REPO` go together. (`POKEMON_IMAGE_BASE` is optional since plan 8L-2: nothing reads it.)
- `ShardDisconnect` logs "shutting down" even when no `onFatal` hook was passed.

- **onnxruntime-node and sharp** (Phase 7): npm 11 runs no install scripts of dependencies until they are approved (`npm approve-scripts`); neither needs its script for the CPU (sharp's prebuilt binary comes as an optional package, onnxruntime-node ships its CPU and DirectML libraries). onnxruntime-node's script downloads the CUDA provider (about 260 MB) on Linux x64: only a GPU image approves it. The image must keep `node_modules/onnxruntime-node/bin` for its platform only (about 44 MB on Linux x64) and `var/ocr/` writable (the models, about 16 MB, are downloaded there at first use).

## Production database findings (2026-10-10)

Checked a backup of the Pittsburgh production database (MySQL 8.0 on Windows) against `test/fixtures/legacy-schema.sql`.

- The schema matches: 15 of 17 tables are identical in columns, keys and collation (`utf8mb4_0900_ai_ci`), and all 27 legacy migrations are present under the same names. Only the two knex bookkeeping tables differ, and the new bot only reads them. The "Production schema check" item under "Needs Matt" is done.
- **Production table names are all lower case** (`guild`, `gymmeta`, `pokemonnotification`, …), because MySQL on Windows lower-cases them. The new bot's queries use the mixed-case names (`Guild`, `GymMeta`), which Windows MySQL accepts but Linux MySQL does not.
- **Decision (Matt): production moves to Linux/Docker.** So the move needs a step that makes the names canonical. For Phase 8 (cutover) and Phase 9 (packaging): add a rename-on-import step (17 `RENAME TABLE` statements, lower case to the names in the fixture) to the cutover tooling, make the baseline migration recognise an all-lower-case legacy schema on a case-sensitive server and tell the operator to run it, and rehearse with the production backup restored into the Linux test container. Do not rely on `lower_case_table_names=1`, which can only be set when a MySQL data directory is first created.
- Volumes, for sizing tests (counted exactly in a restore of the dump of 2026-10-14; the first figures were estimates): 3,213 gyms, 2,057 users, 5,016 Pokémon notifications, 2,120 gym notifications, 28 regions, 4 servers, 35 self-assignable roles, 94 saved routes.
- **The production host** (the old host, seen over SSH on 2026-10-15): an old Windows desktop whose graphics card is too old for CUDA (OCR on the CPU). Legacy Pine runs as the FireDaemon service "Pine" from `<legacy bot folder>` on Node 18, at `main-dev` `f600adc` with local changes not in git (`app/gym.js`, `data/settings.json`, untracked `app/find-raid.js` and a `Dockerfile`): commit them to the legacy branch before the machine is wiped. MySQL 8.0 with `lower_case_table_names=1`; a nightly task dumps `pine` to `C:\backups` (the newest three are kept; 2.8 MB each). Matt will wipe it and install Debian 13; copies of the three dumps are on the laptop in `C:\Users\<you>\pine-backups` (outside the repository; member data), and the newest restored cleanly into MySQL 8.0 on 2026-10-15.

## From Phase 1A (data access and time parsing, 2026-10-10)

Phase 1A was executed from `2026-10-10-phase-1a-data-access-and-time.md`; every task and the whole-branch review passed. Open items, by the plan that should pick them up:

**Phase 1B (game data, raid bosses)**
- New tables state charset and collation explicitly (`utf8mb4`, `utf8mb4_0900_ai_ci`, which is what production uses).
- Use `inTransaction()` (`src/db/transaction.ts`) for multi-step writes so repositories compose inside one transaction (for example replacing the boss list).
- New snowflake columns: decide one column type. `Region.channelId` is BIGINT; `Guild.snowflake` and `User.userSnowflake` are VARCHAR. Compare through `snowflakeParam()` (`src/db/snowflake.ts`) either way.

**Phase 1C (search, image cache, maps)**
- `GymMeta.keywords` written by the legacy importer is free-form text, not necessarily comma-separated. Index it as text; do not rely on `Gym.keywords` being individual terms.
- Loading gyms per region: for 56 regions, `listInRegion` plus `listInPolygon` reads each of about 3,300 gyms several times, including the large `geodata` and `places` columns. Prefer one `listAll()` plus id-only membership queries, or one region-to-gym-ids query.
- `GymMeta.geodata` is a JSON string with more than one legacy shape (`app/gym.js:292-296`, `app/geocode.js:150-154`, `importgyms.js:204`). Whatever the new bot writes must not break the legacy index build on rollback; consider having `GymRepository` own the parsing.
- Neighbouring-region gyms: the legacy bot scaled a region's polygon by 1.5 (`app/region.js` `enlargePolygonFromRegion`); build it on `GymRepository.listInPolygon`.

**Phase 2 (raids on forums)**
- `resolvePartyTime` accepts `null` or 0 for absent party times and durations. The legacy `'unset'` end-time string must be mapped to null by whoever imports legacy parties (Phase 8).
- Commit an end-to-end legacy parity fixture for `resolvePartyTime` (the reviewers' ad hoc differentials of 15,000+ cases against `types/time.js` found no conflicting times, but they are not in the repo). `scripts/legacy-parity/moment-time-cases.ts` shows the method.
- When doing that, also pin the knock-on effects of the stricter duration grammar that no fixture entry covers yet: on a raid, `3.30` and `13.50` are now clock times (they were "3 minutes from now"), and `120m` is now 120 minutes (it was 1:20).
- `gym-repository.db.test.ts`: the transaction commit test leaves a region behind and the rollback test passes only because it runs first; clear `Region` in `beforeEach` when that file is next touched.
- The commands render the `out-of-range` result as "valid between `first` and `last`".
- Add `RegionRepository.findById` and a `regionsContaining(point, guildId)` that returns `Region` rows: `Party.regionId` needs the row id, and `channelsContaining` returns only channel IDs.
- Logging convention: never log `ctx`, config or env objects. Redaction (`src/log.ts`) is a safety net that reaches one level down only.

**Phase 4 (notifications and members)**
- Add `UserRepository.getMany(snowflakes)` (the legacy `/shout` ran one query per attendee) and an id-to-snowflake lookup for notification joins.
- The in-game nickname lookup is case- and accent-insensitive (the column's collation); say so in the command's help.

**Phase 5 (admin)**
- Validate text length before saving a gym: `he.encode` expands text (an apostrophe becomes six characters; non-Latin names much more) against `varchar(255)`.
- `RegionRepository.saveBounds` is select-then-insert with no unique key on `Region.channelId`, as in the legacy bot; two concurrent imports for a new channel could both insert. Decide whether to add a unique key.
- Keyword de-duplication when adding keywords.
- Check the production backup for `GymMeta` rows whose nickname or description is the literal `skip`, `n` or `null` (the legacy display code guards against them, which suggests they exist); the new repository would show them as real text.

**Time parsing: deliberate differences from the legacy bot** (all tested; the lists in `src/domain/time/clock-input.test.ts` are the record)
- For standard raids only, a date-less time also tries tomorrow and yesterday, and `12:xx` also tries `00:xx`, so times around midnight work.
- A relative duration must be a number of minutes or hours (`45`, `30 minutes`, `2 hours`, `1:30`); other digit-led text is refused instead of being read as minutes.
- A well-formed duration that is too long reports the valid window instead of "not a time".
- A leading "at" is accepted for trains and meetups too; `3.30pm` and `10-14 at 6pm` are accepted.

## Onboarding ideas (Matt, 2026-10-10; for the later sub-project in spec section 10)

- **Regions:** probably a small web interface served by Pine itself, where an admin draws region borders on a map, instead of drawing on a Google Map and exporting KML. To be revisited as an onboarding enhancement; `/region import kml` (Phase 5) stays the baseline.
  - Superseded 2026-10-15 by the spec `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-15-web-map-region-editor-design.md` (plans 8M-1 to 8M-3, before the cutover, after 8W-1): Pine's own web server with Discord login, a public live map (regions and gyms for all, the parties a logged-in member can see, with joining) and a region editor for staff that saves through `RegionAdminService`. Extended the same day to plans 8M-4 (raids and spawns from the map) and 8M-5 (trains and meetups from the map).
- **Scopely Campfire** (Matt, 2026-10-15): integrate `https://campfire.scopely.com/discover` if possible; it seems to list only meetups now, which limits its use. Campfire is the game's official social app (Android and iOS; Scopely, formerly Niantic), and its web side is new and growing, so what it offers may change. A research spike first (what the site offers, its terms, whether a logged-in account may read it, as for Wayfarer); nothing planned.
- **Research tasks** (Matt, 2026-10-15, for their own design after 8W-2): the task list comes from ScrapedDuck (its research data, as the raid bosses do). Sponsored PokéStops are not in Wayfarer, so reporting tasks needs staff to add such stops by hand (a stop of Pine's own, which imports never delete, like a gym marked "not in Wayfarer").
- **Gyms:** Matt will look for a better way to import the known gyms of a region, possibly using the Wayfarer site (`https://wayfarer.scopely.com/`). Pine-GymReader is not an option (see above).

## From Phase 1B-1 (game data catalogue, 2026-10-10)

Phase 1B-1 was executed from `2026-10-10-phase-1b1-game-data.md`; every task and the whole-branch review passed. The catalogue holds 1,456 entries (1,024 species, regional and other forms, 61 megas) and 328 moves from the game master of 2026-10-10. Open items, by the plan that should pick them up:

**Decided by Matt (2026-10-10): one identity per form**
- (The count and the exact rule were worked out in the 1B-2 plan, section "Decisions made in this plan": 25 species, not 26.) 26 species have a named default form that is identical to the species entry (`landorus` and `landorus_incarnate`, `giratina` and `giratina_altered`, Tornadus, Thundurus, Enamorus, Hoopa, Aegislash, Lycanroc, …). **The form is the identity:** the plain species entry is collapsed into the named default form, so the catalogue has `landorus_incarnate` ("Landorus (Incarnate Forme)") and no separate `landorus`. The plain species id, and every other form id that is not its own entry (`_NORMAL`, costumes, cosmetic forms), become aliases that resolve to the entry they stand for.
- Consequences for the 1B-2 plan, which makes this change first: `GameData` gains the alias map; `byKey` / `byGameMasterId` resolve aliases (so `LANDORUS`, the legacy name `landorus`, and a costume such as `CORSOLA_SPRING_2026` → `corsola_galarian` all land on the right entry); `catalogue.species(dex)` returns the default-form entry; `keyForLegacyName` / `legacyNameForKey` and the parity test follow the aliases; `CURATED_NICKNAMES` for the 26 twins are merged onto the form key; bump `GAME_DATA_VERSION` and regenerate the fixtures once. Species whose only entry is the plain one (most of them) are unchanged.

**Phase 1B-2 (raid bosses)**
- Done (see "From Phase 1B-2" below). Still open from this list: `GameDataProvider` exposes only `catalogue`, so the 1C search index must notice a refresh itself (compare the catalogue object, or add a revision number).

**Phase 1C (search, image cache, maps)**
- Search names for a Pokémon are its display name, its key, `CURATED_NICKNAMES` and `PokemonRepository.nicknames()`. The repository also returns keys that are not Pokémon (`ex`, `perfect`, `zero`, `undefined`): ignore keys the catalogue does not have.
- Six curated nicknames are class words shared by many Pokémon (`mega` on 48, `alo` 18, `kanto` 18, `galar` 15, `hisu` 11): index them as extra search terms, not as aliases of one Pokémon.
- About 200 form entries have the same stats, types and moves as their species (Vivillon patterns, Unown letters, Spinda, Furfrou, Flabébé colours). Collapse them in search results unless the query names the form.
- Display names are unique in today's data (a test pins the fixture), but the parser does not enforce it: break ties by key.
- 17 species, 11 megas and 13 moves have fallback names until the text file catches up, so a name can change on a later refresh: never store a name. Move names are not unique (Weather Ball ×5, Techno Blast ×5).
- The legacy search also matched the boss CP (`raidBossCp`) and bare tier digits; the notification targets had nicknames too (`hundo` for perfect; `none`, `useless` for zero; `ex`, `exclusive` for EX).
- Icons: `GameData` holds no icon ids. Either derive file names the way the feed does (`pm<dex>[.f<FORM>].icon.png`) or add the asset ids from `formSettings` and `temporaryEvolutionSettings` to the parser.
- Rebuild the index when the catalogue changes (see the change-signal bullet under 1B-2).

**Phase 2 (raids on forums)**
- `raidDurationKind(tier)` gives the `RaidKind` that `resolvePartyTime` needs.
- `catalogue.boostedIn` lists weather in a fixed order, not in the order of the Pokémon's types as the legacy bot did; the renderer decides the display order.
- A costume boss is shown under its species name (costumes are not catalogue entries; the spec's section 6.6 mentioned costume display names, which this plan deliberately does not provide).

**Phase 4 (notifications)**
- **Decided by Matt (2026-10-10):** a `-5` want means ordinary tier 5 raids only. The legacy bot also fired it for elite raids with a known boss and for shadow tier 5 raids, but most people want just normal tier 5; elite, EX and shadow tier 5 are separate wants (the codec already has their own codes). So the matcher does not widen existing `-5` rows, and members who want shadow tier 5 or elite alerts add those wants. Worth one line in the cutover announcement.
- `PokemonNotification.type` holds `raid`, `spawn` or `both` in any letter case (2,731 of the 5,016 production rows are capitalised): read it only through `parseNotificationScope` or `decodeNotification`. `decodeNotification` returns undefined for rows it does not understand (a legacy tier row typed `spawn`, including the stray `(-7, spawn)` row; since Phase 1B-2 `(-7, raid)` is the super mega tier): skip those, never delete them.
- The new bot writes tier wants with type `raid` and IV wants with type `spawn`, whatever the member picked. A legacy row `(-6, both)` is a perfect-IV want (a 2019 legacy migration created them); production had no mega-legendary wants as of 2026-10. It would help if `decodeNotification` also returned the effective scope.
- The legacy bot matched a notification's Pokédex number against every form of the species; keep that.

**Phase 5 (admin)**
- `/admin boss sync` cannot reach `GameDataService.refresh()`, `fetchedAt` or the last failure through `ctx.gameData`, which is only a `GameDataProvider`. Widen the context type or pass the service to the command.
- `PokemonRepository.addNickname` creates a missing row with the column defaults (see Phase 8).

**Phase 6 (utilities)**
- `pokebattlerId` reproduces the legacy names apart from three legacy errors, but neither was checked against Pokebattler itself: verify a few forms against the live API when `/counters` is ported.
- `/rank … evolutions`: `PokemonEntry.family` is the whole game-master family (the legacy data had per-regional-line families for 126 entries), and `evolutionBranch` is not parsed. Add `evolutions` (keys) to the parser.
- `/rank` needs CP at half levels (`cpMultiplierAt` supports them). The game master lists 80 levels: cap the level. The legacy bot rounded CP multipliers to 32-bit floats; without that, CP differs by 1 in a tiny share of combinations (none at raid levels).

**Phase 8 (cutover and rollback)**
- Rollback: before starting the legacy bot again, delete `PokemonNotification` rows with `pokemon < -8` (the new elite, EX and shadow tier codes) and the rows with `pokemon = -7` typed `raid` (super mega; in the legacy bot 7 was the "rare" marker); one such row breaks the legacy `!wants` listing for that member.
- Rollback: a `Pokemon` row the new bot created for a nickname has tier 0 and no flags, which makes the legacy bot stop treating that Pokémon as a raid boss if its boss status came only from `data/pokemon.json` (in production: `venusaur_mega`, `blastoise_mega`). Check rows created after cutover.
- Delete `data/pokemon.json`, `data/types.json`, `data/weather.json`, the parity test that reads `pokemon.json`, and `scripts/game-data/make-nicknames.ts` together with the legacy tree.
- Basculegion and the Hisuian Sliggoo and Goodra were in the legacy data but are not in the game master; nothing to migrate, but mention it in the release notes.

**Phase 9 (packaging)**
- The parsed game data is cached at `var/game-data-cache.json`, relative to the working directory: it belongs on the `var/` volume. The first start needs network access; later starts work offline.

**Game data upkeep (any phase)**
- `parseGameMaster` reports what it drops through `onWarning` and refuses a game master with no Pokémon, moves, form settings, weather, type chart or CP multipliers. `GameDataService` also refuses a refresh with far fewer species than it has, or with a text file that names fewer than half of them, and keeps the data in use. A new temporary-evolution id (beyond MEGA, MEGA_X, MEGA_Y, PRIMAL) is reported and skipped, so a new kind of mega needs a code change.
- Bump `GAME_DATA_VERSION` (`src/domain/pogo/game-master.ts`) on any change to what the parser produces; a test pins the shape to it. A saved cache with another version is discarded.
- Refresh the test fixtures with `npm run fixtures:game-data -- --download`; the script's header says which test expectations may move when the game rebalances.
- Megas are read only from a species' formless item; none are declared elsewhere today.
- Left open by the final review (all minor): at start-up with an out-of-date saved copy, the "far fewer species" check has nothing to compare with, so a truncated download is accepted until the next refresh (pass the saved copy as the baseline); `npm run fixtures:game-data -- --download` overwrites the text file before fetching the game master, so a failed second download leaves a mismatched pair in `var/game-data-source/`; a few comment lines in `game-data-service.ts` run over 100 characters.
- A costume that copies a regional form (`CORSOLA_SPRING_2026` is Galarian Corsola) is not an entry, and `byGameMasterId` then falls back to the plain species, which has other types. The alias map decided above fixes this.

## From Phase 1B-2 (raid bosses, 2026-10-10)

Phase 1B-2 was executed from `2026-10-10-phase-1b2-raid-bosses.md`; every task and the whole-branch review passed, and `npm run raid-bosses:check` read the live feed (14 bosses in 7 tiers, all resolved). The catalogue now has 1,431 entries and 133 aliases; 25 species are known by their default form. Open items, by the plan that should pick them up:

**Needs Matt**
- Super mega raids (settled by Matt, 2026-10-10): tier 7, 25,000 stamina whether or not the boss is legendary (Bulbapedia, "Raid Battle (GO)"), so one `SuperMega` level is enough. For the record, the game master names two levels, `RAID_LEVEL_4_MEGA_ENHANCED` and `RAID_LEVEL_5_MEGA_ENHANCED`; the feed has one key, `super_mega`. Their durations are assumed to be the standard ones.
- The "enraged" mechanic of super mega raids (a shield that takes a number of attacks from a mega Pokémon to break) is not in the game master: the only traces are the texts `battle_condition_enrage` ("{0} became enraged!") and `battle_condition_suppress` ("{0} has been subdued!"), which shadow raids also use. The number of attacks is decided by the server. Nothing in Pine depends on it today.

**Checked against Bulbapedia, "Raid Battle (GO)" (2026-10-10)**
- Pine's boss stamina per level, boss CP and catch CP agree with the page (checked for eleven bosses of every level). Two things were wrong and are fixed: a shadow raid catch has IVs of at least 6, not 10 (`catalogue.raidCatchCp(entry, shadow)`), and a fused boss (White and Black Kyurem, Dusk Mane and Dawn Wings Necrozma, the Calyrex riders) is caught as its base Pokémon, like a mega (`FUSED_FORMS` in `catalogue.ts`; add new fusions there).
- Raid kinds Pine has no level for, none of them in the feed: Community Day raids (four-star, 9,000 stamina, local only), which Matt says seem to have been retired (2026-10-10), and Unity raids (local only, more than 20 players). Left out on purpose.
- One event used another stamina than its level: Mega Rayquaza in elite raids had 22,500, not 20,000, so Pine would show a boss CP about 6% low for a mega boss in an elite raid. Not handled; Matt (2026-10-10): not a big deal for a one-time past event.
- Primal raids are classed as five-star in the game although they have the mega legendary stamina; Pine files them under Mega Legendary, so a tier 5 want does not fire for them. Matt (2026-10-10): leave as it is for now and decide in Phase 4.
- Phase 2, times: an egg normally takes 60 minutes and a boss stays 45, but Raid Hour bosses stay 25 minutes, Raid Day bosses 60, and an elite boss 30 or 45 after an egg of up to 24 hours. A hatched duration longer than the configured one is refused by `resolvePartyTime` today, as in the legacy bot. **Matt's direction (2026-10-10): detect it automatically.** Raid Hour is every Wednesday 18:00 to 19:00 local time (two rounds of 25-minute bosses, at 18:00 and 18:30); Raid Days and similar events would have to come from an events feed (Leek Duck's events; ScrapedDuck publishes them as JSON; whether pokemon-go-api has an events file is not checked yet). The Phase 2 plan should investigate the feed and design this: the raid durations in effect at a given time, with the configured ones as the fallback.
- Phase 2, groups: a lobby holds 20 players. Phase 2, display: pass `tier.shadow` to `raidCatchCp`.

**Checked against the production backup (2026-10-10)**
- Of the bare names the legacy data file does not have (`giratina`, `shaymin`, `cherrim`, `zacian`, `zamazenta`, `wormadam`), only a `zacian` row exists in `Pokemon`, with no nickname. All six bare names now map to the default form in `LEGACY_NAME_ALIASES`, so such a row is read as the form and a nickname added for the form is found on it. No row was changed: the legacy bot still reads this table until cutover. Phase 8 may rename such rows for tidiness (`zacian` to `zacian_hero` cannot be done while a `zacian_hero` row exists: the name is unique; merge the nicknames and delete the stray row instead).

**Phase 1C (search, image cache, maps)**
- Look Pokémon up through `catalogue.byKey` and keep the `key` of the entry returned: `landorus`, `giratina` and costume ids are aliases now. No entry is named plain "Landorus" or "Giratina" any more, so the bare species name must be an extra search term for the 25 default forms (and an OCR name in Phase 7: the raid screen shows the bare name).
- `PokemonRepository.nicknames()` is keyed by entry keys for every name in the legacy data file; a row under another legacy name could still come back under an alias, so merge under `catalogue.byKey(key)?.key`.
- `PokemonRepository.findRow` takes the lowest id when a key has two legacy rows (now also Pumpkaboo, Gourgeist, Hoopa, Palafin): removing a nickname that sits on the other row reports false.

**Phase 2 (raids on forums)**
- An egg of a tier hatches as `ctx.raidBosses.autoset(tier)` (the tier's only boss, or the moderators' choice). A raid's boss is stored by catalogue key, or by the feed's name when `entry` is undefined. Boss CP is `raidBossCp(entry.stats, bossStamina(tier))`.
- Escape a boss `name` before showing it: for a boss the game master does not know it is the feed's text (control characters are already removed, Markdown is not).
- Forum tags (spec 5.1) have no tag for super mega raids: decide one.

**Phase 4 (notifications)**
- `/want` offers the super mega tier (code -7). Matching a want's Pokédex number against a boss uses `entry.dex`; a boss without an entry matches nobody.

**Phase 5 (admin)**
- `/admin boss` (list, add, hide, autoset, shiny, remove, sync) on `RaidBossService.addOverride` / `removeOverride` / `overrides()` / `sync()`; take the ids from `bossIdsOf(entry)`; turn the `RangeError` of `addOverride` into a `UserError`. The command needs the service itself: `ctx.raidBosses` is only a `RaidBossProvider`. Write overrides only through the repository (the table has no unique key).
- Post `onChange` to bot-updates when `settings.raidBosses.announceChanges`, and `onStale` to the mod bot channel. Attach the listeners before `raidBosses.startAutoSync()` in `src/app.ts`, and post only once the client is ready: `login()` resolves before `ClientReady`, so either queue the events or move the call into the ready handler.
- An announcement gets raw `FeedBoss` rows: resolve each through the catalogue for its name, escape the feed's name when it does not resolve, and drop add/remove pairs that resolve to the same Pokémon and tier (a feed that only renames a costume id).
- Tell moderators about level keys the feed has and Pine does not know (today only a log line): add them to `SyncResult` or an event. They are the only sign that a tier vanished because the feed renamed a key; consider flagging a sync that drops most of the list.
- The stale state is per process: a bot with an old list warns again after each restart, and after a long stop one failed first attempt reports stale at once. Say since when, and consider waiting for a second failure. The stale `reason` can be a raw database message: do not post it as it is.
- An `autoset` override without expiry outlives the rotation (the Pokémon stays a boss of the tier): default an expiry or show old ones in the list. An override for a Pokémon the game master does not know has no name (it shows as the title-cased id).
- A costume newer than the game master does not resolve for the 25 default-form species (the feed says "Landorus", the entry is "Landorus (Incarnate Forme)"): it is listed under the feed's name without stats until the game data catches up. Rows edited by hand with lower-case ids only half work (the catalogue look-up and the feed comparison are case-sensitive); the code never writes such rows.

**Phase 6 (utilities)**
- The Pokebattler name of 14 default forms is now `<FORM>_FORM` where the legacy data had the bare species id (`LEGACY_SPECIES_ID_FOR_DEFAULT_FORM` in `legacy-names.test.ts`); check which Pokebattler accepts.

**Phase 8 (cutover and rollback)**
- Rollback needs nothing for the two new tables (the legacy bot does not know them). On a Linux import the lower-case table names to rename include `raidboss` and `raidbossoverride` if the new bot ever ran against the Windows database: derive the list from the schema, not from a count.
- The shutdown steps may take up to 16 seconds in total; a supervisor's grace period (Docker's default is 10) must allow it.

**Upkeep (any phase)**
- `parseGameMaster` warns when a species whose forms differ keeps a bare entry: add it to `DEFAULT_FORMS` or `NO_DEFAULT_FORM` (`game-master.ts`). A test pins the 25 default forms; a change there changes keys, so check `LEGACY_NAME_ALIASES` and bump `GAME_DATA_VERSION` if the rule changed.
- `RaidBossService` has no overall deadline for a store call that hangs, reads a response body before checking its size, and never deletes retired `RaidBoss` rows (a few hundred a year). `MemoryRaidBossStore` re-implements the repository's rules; a shared contract suite run against both would stop them drifting.
- `npm run raid-bosses:check` shows what Pine makes of the live feed; the test fixture is the feed of 2026-10-10 and is not refreshed by it.

## From Phase 1C (search, image cache, static maps, 2026-10-10)

Phase 1C was executed from `2026-10-10-phase-1c-search-images-maps.md`; every task and the whole-branch review passed (773 unit tests, 97 database tests). This finishes Phase 1. The code differs from the plan's text where the reviews changed it; the differences are listed under "As built (Phase 1C)" in spec section 4.3.

**Measured on a scratch copy of the production gyms (3,213 gyms, 18,087 searches made from their names; aggregates only)**

- The gym meant was returned in every search. By full name it came first in about 3,010 of the 3,090 cases where the legacy search had it first.
- With a letter missing or two letters swapped, it was found in all 1,406 and 1,255 cases where the legacy search found nothing. With the last word half typed (what autocomplete sees) it was found in all 2,824 cases the legacy search missed.
- A search takes 4 ms at the median and under 25 ms at worst; the index builds in about 0.3 s.
- 411 gyms lie in no region (they are found only from a channel that is not a region, or as neighbours). Worth a look when regions are next edited (Phase 5).

**Phase 2 (raids on forums)**

- `gym` autocomplete and the free-text fallback (spec 4.3) on `ctx.gyms.search(channelId, text, { limit: 25 })`. Always pass `limit`: a single digit or a common word matches a great many gyms. Map a forum post or thread to its region's channel id before calling.
- What a hit means: `allWords: false` is a guess; `corrected: true` needed a misspelling; `place: 'neighbouring'` is the "gym lies in an adjacent region" case that asks for confirmation (`ctx.gyms.regionsOf(gym.id)` gives the region channels). Act silently only on a hit that is `allWords`, not `corrected`, and clearly ahead; `score` is comparable only between hits with the same `place` and `corrected` of one search.
- Known order quirk: the retry that ignores an unfinished single letter is made per place, so from a region channel `lot b` can put a local "Lot A" before a neighbouring "Lot B". A match found by that retry is not marked.
- A single letter typed alone matches only a gym with that one-letter word, so the first keystroke of autocomplete shows nothing. Decide whether to show recent or nearby gyms instead.
- `boss` autocomplete lists bosses and eggs from the boss list (tiers, shadow and CP come from there, not from the Pokémon search). For free text: `ctx.pokemon.exact(text)` first; otherwise offer `ctx.pokemon.search(text)` as a choice. `exact` is undefined for a Pokédex number (although `search` puts that species first) and defined for the single letters `a` to `z` (the Unown letters): do not act on a one-letter text.
- Extra words in a Pokémon search find nothing (`the giratina`, `shadow mewtwo`, `raid`): strip tier and shadow words before searching. On the misspelt path a form can come before its species (`zacain`).
- Moves: `parseMoveset` takes the first of several equal fits (`chop`, `punch`) without saying so; for autocomplete use `findMoves` and offer the list.
- Maps: `ctx.maps.gym(gym)` may be undefined, and the file it names can be deleted a moment later by a newer picture of the same gym: read the file and treat a missing one as "no map". Do not hold the post back for the map: while Google is slow each request waits up to 10 seconds, and nothing remembers a recent failure.
- Pokémon icons: take the address from the raid boss feed (`assets.image`, `assets.shinyImage`; not parsed yet). The public icon files cannot be derived from ids (84% at best).
- Test support: `createTestContext()` now has `gyms`, `pokemon` (built on first use from the context's own game data) and `maps`.

**Phase 4 (notifications)**

- The legacy words for notification targets (`hundo` for perfect; `none`, `useless` for zero; `ex`, `exclusive`) if `/want` takes free text.

**Phase 5 (admin)**

- Call `GymDirectory.refreshGym(id)` after every gym edit, `load()` after a region import, and `PokemonSearchService.reloadNicknames()` after a nickname change; these need the services themselves, not the `ctx` interfaces. `reloadNicknames` is not serialised: of two overlapping calls the read that finishes last wins.
- `/admin clear-image-cache` on `ImageCache.clear()`. Pictures of deleted gyms and regions, and a `*.tmp` file left by a crash, are never removed otherwise.
- Geocoding and Places (what fills `geodata` and `places`): keep writing `{"addressComponents": {…}}`.
- Warn when a gym's whole name consists of ignored words (it can then be found only by its number). Initials typed without dots (`tj maxx` for "T.J. Maxx") are only a guess.
- With an unwritable `var/images`, every map request downloads again and logs a warning; check the folder at start or in the health check (Phase 9: the container user must be able to write `var/`).

**Phase 6 (utilities)**

- `/find-region`: maps of arbitrary shapes are `staticMapQuery({ paths, markers })`, downloaded without caching; real geometry (intersections) needs turf, which this phase did not add.

**Phase 7 (OCR)**

- `nameOnly` searching is there; the bare species name on the raid screen is found by the Pokémon search.

**Left as they are**

- Searches from a channel that is not a region look at the gyms of all guilds (the legacy behaviour; production has one guild). The gym notice is not searched. Unown's "!" and "?" forms are found by key, nickname or the words "question" / "exclamation".
- A typo in a half-typed word finds nothing until the word is nearly complete (the index has no misspelt beginnings). "saint" and "st." are different words. Cached map pictures never expire.
- A point exactly on a region's outline counts as inside in memory and outside for MySQL's `ST_Contains`; no real gym is that exact.
- `parseStatusWord`, `parsePrivacyWord` and `parseMoveset` may have few callers once options are slash-command choices; they are kept for free-text input and OCR.

## From Phase 2A (command framework, 2026-10-11)

As built: commits `15a1b7f`..`fa6789e` (six tasks, a formatting fix, one fix wave after the whole-branch review); 859 unit tests, 97 database tests. The code of the six tasks equals the prototype.

**Smoke start, 2026-10-11 (the bot's first connection to Discord), on the test application and Matt's test server "Googer":**

- Worked: the guild-scoped registration (Discord accepts `contexts` and `integration_types` in the body and ignores them for a guild command); login without privileged intents; the game data download; the raid boss feed (14 bosses); `/help` and `/help command:` with autocomplete, answered privately.
- The application's own install link grants only `applications.commands`, so the command appeared in the server while the bot had not joined (it could not see the server: 404). The invite must carry `scope=bot+applications.commands`; add `&guild_id=<id>&disable_guild_select=true` to preselect the server. Worth a line in the setup guide (Phase 9).
- The bot is also in "the second development server" from long ago; nothing is registered there.
- `/help` in a DM worked after a global registration (Matt had to reload his Discord client to see the new command); the global copy was removed again afterwards, so the test application has only the guild registration.
- Graceful shutdown on Windows: Ctrl+C under `npm run dev` (`tsx watch`) ends the bot at once, without the shutdown steps. Tested with a stand-in script and a real console Ctrl+C: run with plain `node`, the script received SIGINT and finished its slow shutdown; under `tsx watch` the signal never reached it. The "Terminate batch job (Y/N)?" prompt is npm's wrapper and says nothing either way. Production (Linux, Docker, `node dist/index.js`, SIGTERM) is the plain-node case, but the bot's own shutdown steps have still not been watched against Discord: do it once with `npm run build` and `npm start`, or in the container (Phase 9).
- Not done: the scaffolding channels (needed from plan 2C); a start against a copy of the legacy database.
- A successful interaction writes no log line, even at `debug`, so the log could not show whether `/help` had arrived. Add one debug line per handled interaction (command path or custom-id kind, user id, milliseconds): plan 2C at the latest.

**Only a real Discord connection can confirm (staging checklist and later):**

- `/help` in a DM needs a global registration: a guild-scoped command never appears in a DM. Registering both ways shows every command twice in the test server; remove the global one afterwards.
- After a public deferral and an error, the "thinking…" message disappears and the follow-up is visible only to the member (first real case: plan 2C).
- A follow-up after `showModal` is accepted (Phase 3/5).
- A button on an archived or never-seen forum post after a restart: `interaction.channel` is filled in and `channelContext` gives the forum as base (plan 2D).
- `requireBotChannel` and `requirePvpContext` inside a real thread and a real forum post.
- `default_member_permissions` hides `/admin`, `/gym` and `/region` from members, consistent with `/help`.
- One deliberately failed reply: the log line must show `err.url` and `err.requestBody` as redacted.
- Autocomplete answers within three seconds with the production gym set (plan 2C).

**Phase 5:**

- `/help command:admin boss` (a group name) answers "There is no /admin boss command."; list the group's subcommands instead.
- Discord limits one command to 8,000 characters over all names, descriptions and choices: add an invariant test in `deploy.test.ts` once `/admin` and `/gym` grow.
- `/gym create|edit` over several modals: a modal cannot follow a modal submit, so each step needs a button in between, and the pending-state store of 2C.
- Autocomplete inside a subcommand group is not yet exercised by a test.

**Small, whenever the file is next touched:**

- The fakes are still looser than discord.js in places: a wrongly typed option returns null, `getInteger`/`getNumber` are interchangeable, every fake has every responder; and `getMember(name, true)` throws although discord.js has no `required` there.
- The `err.cause.url` / `err.cause.requestBody` redaction paths do nothing (pino does not emit `cause` as an object); redaction covers errors logged under `err`.
- The 2,000-character cut of an error text can split a surrogate pair.
- `channel-context.test.ts`: the test title "knows only the id of a channel that is not cached (an archived post)" keeps the wording the doc comment dropped.
- `commandGroup` does not refuse a subcommand and a group of the same name (Discord refuses it at deploy).

## From Phase 2B (party model, table, raid durations, 2026-10-11)

As built: commits `6894f6d`..`061c2e6` (seven tasks and their fix rounds, one fix wave after the whole-branch review); 1,061 unit tests, 119 database tests before the last wave. The plan document shows Tasks 1 and 2 before their fix commits; the code is the authority.

**For Matt to confirm or correct (each is a setting or one line):**

- Settled by Matt (2026-10-11): a boss that hatches during a Raid Hour stays 25 minutes; during a Raid Day 30 minutes, with raids on the half hour through the event (six rounds from 14:00 to 17:00). The weekly Raid Hour of the settings stays as the fallback for a week in which the feed lists none.
- The event duration applies to every standard-kind raid hatching inside the event, whatever its tier; events do not change the incubation time; an event of the feed longer than 24 hours is ignored.
- The weekly Raid Hour (Wednesday 18:00 to 19:00, `settings.raidEvents.weeklyRaidHour`) applies only in a week for which the feed lists no Raid Hour; a week where the feed lists only an extra Raid Hour on another day therefore has no Wednesday one.
- The events feed is `events.min.json` of ScrapedDuck on GitHub (Leek Duck's list). `pokemon-go-api` has no events file.
- Differences from the legacy bot, all deliberate (plan 2B, decisions 3 to 7): `/done`, `/not-here` and `−1` from somebody not signed up are refused; a second report of a raid does not reset the reporter's sign-up; "done" asks the others only when the caller was coming, present or asked; a member who was asked and moves to another group counts as "not yet"; a raid that has ended by the clock no longer blocks a new report for its gym; a corrected time calls a scheduled archiving off; an egg gets its auto-set boss when it hatches, not when it is reported.

**Still open after plan 2D-2:**

- A save that never settles blocks that party's queue (no time limit in `KeyedLock`).
- The legacy parity fixture for `resolvePartyTime` is still open ("From Phase 1A").

**Phase 3 (trains and meetups):**

- `Party` is `RaidParty` only; the table and the store take other types, and a row of type `train` is reported as unreadable until the union and the state schema know it. `remoteAllowed`, `requireParty`'s text and `tick.ts` (typed for raids) are the places the compiler or a reader will point at.

**Left as they are:**

- Three result shapes in the domain (`Change`, `{ party, ... }`, a bare party); `PartyStore.update` accepts the first two, and the compiler refuses a bare party.
- Immutability is by convention (the types are not `readonly` all the way down).
- `listEndedBefore` (for the retention sweep) uses `updatedAt` as the time of ending, which holds because the store stamps every save.
- The events service keeps events in memory only; after a restart the weekly rule applies until the first read.

## From Phase 2C (forum posts, `/admin setup-forums`, `/raid`, 2026-10-11)

As built: commits `0b8b427`..`0efd6e7` (nine tasks, three fix rounds after the task reviews, one after the whole-branch review, one after the staging run); 1,323 unit tests, 121 database tests. The plan document shows the tasks before the fix rounds; the code is the authority.

**Staging run, 2026-10-11, on the test application and the server "Googer" (Matt's go-ahead; the notes are in the git-ignored `.superpowers/sdd/2026-10-11-phase-2c-forum-posts/staging-notes.md`):**

- Worked on first contact: the guild registration of `/help`, `/raid`, `/admin`; `setupForums` (a forum on a server that is not a community server, twelve moderated tags, a pinned summary, nothing changed on a second run) with the bot's role having Manage Channels and Manage Threads but not Administrator; reporting an egg and a boss raid (title, tags, embed with the boss's picture, link line, summary); a second report of the same gym; edits; our own rename budget holding back a third rename; archived posts reopened on the next change; a deleted first message (Discord deletes the whole thread with it) ending the party; a deleted summary replaced; the real bot's start and its graceful shutdown (seven steps).
- Scaffolding left on the test server: category "Pine Test" with `#test-region`, `#the-bot-lab` and the forum `test-region-raids`. The local database has that region and eight invented gyms.
- Still only Matt can check, with the bot running (`npm run dev`): how a post reads, also for members his client has not loaded; the `/raid` autocomplete lists and their speed; the gym menu (`gym:pinewood` fits two gyms) and the confirmation for a gym of another region; that errors after the private "thinking…" stay private; that `/admin` is hidden from members without Manage Server and `/admin setup-forums` answers "is ready".
- Not seen yet: Discord's own rename limit (what it puts into `sublimitTimeout`, `timeToReset`, `retryAfter`), because our budget kept clear of it; a second pin in one forum; a forum under a category that has permission overwrites.

**Decided by Matt (2026-10-11):**

- Lists that do not fit are cut with a count; no second message for very large raids.
- Mega legendary and super mega raids use the `Mega` tag.
- Team emoji without a privileged intent: note a member's team (the role named instinct, mystic or valor) and display name from the interaction when they sign up or act, store both with the attendee, group by team and sort by name; a member without a team has no emoji. Done in plan 2D-1 (see "From Phase 2D-1").
- The raid boss list comes from ScrapedDuck's `raids.json` alone; pokemon-go-api is dropped. Done in plan 2C-2 (see "From Phase 2C-2").

**For Matt to decide:**

- Members can create posts in a raid forum by hand: the forum takes the permissions of its category and nothing more. Deny "Send Messages" (which in a forum is "Create Posts") for everyone and allow "Send Messages in Threads"? `/admin setup-forums` could set that.
- The tags are moderated (members cannot apply them), so the bot needs Manage Threads for every report, not only for the setup.
- A typed boss that is not on the list needs its tier first (`t3 pikachu`); an unlisted mega of a legendary Pokémon is filed as Mega.

**Still open after plan 2D-2:**

- A hint when a second report's boss or time is dropped because the raid exists.
- A post made by a report that was cut off before it was recorded stays open: the sweep of plan 2D-2 touches only posts of ended parties.

**Left as it is (plan 2E-1):** autocomplete runs no guards, so a member can see gym names and a region's channel name from any channel; gyms are public knowledge.

**Phase 7 (OCR):** the screenshot option of `/raid`.

**Small, whenever the file is next touched:**

- The debug line per interaction could say the outcome (ok, refused, failed).
- `/raid`'s gym menu and `gymChoices` name a gym's region from `regionsOf(gym)[0]`, not from where the raid would go.
- One unit test is flaky under load: `game-data-service.test.ts`, "retries soon after a stale start".
- The Done field's shortening when an embed exceeds 6,000 characters has no test (the group fields always make room first).

## From Phase 2C-2 (raid bosses from ScrapedDuck, 2026-10-11)

As built: commits `9f92503`..`e8a4d1b` (two tasks and three fix rounds after reviews that probed the reader with made-up entries, Leek Duck's real file names, and finally every entry of the catalogue); 1,503 unit tests. The live feed reads as the same 14 bosses in 7 tiers as before. The plan was not prototyped (a small change behind an unchanged interface); the price was three fix rounds on the matching rule, which ended as one rule: picture and name must not contradict each other.

**Watch when the rotation changes (only Leek Duck can show these):**

- The headings of the rarer kinds: mega legendary, super mega, elite, primal, EX, and whether shadow raids ever get headings of their own. The reader knows the likely spellings; anything else appears in the log as a level Pine does not know and in `npm run raid-bosses:check`, and its bosses are missing until the table in `raid-boss-feed.ts` learns the heading.
- How Leek Duck names forms: `Deoxys (Normal)`, `Wormadam (Plant)`, a bare `Landorus` are read; other short spellings may leave a boss unresolved (shown by name, without stats) until `findByName`'s respellings learn them. Run the check script after a rotation with unusual forms.
- A costume without a year in its picture's file name (the old `pikachu_doctor` style) stays unresolved until the game master knows it.

**Phase 5 (`/admin boss`):**

- An unresolved boss's key can equal a real catalogue key (`Genesect (Burn)` with a formless picture is stored as `GENESECT_BURN` without a form): an override for the real Pokémon and tier is then confused with it. Show unresolved bosses in `/admin boss list` with their ids.
- Two unresolved bosses that share a picture of an unknown form become one row.
- `/admin boss list` should show how each boss was identified, as the check script does.

**Small:** the costume rule accepts any digit in a form part (`f10_PERCENT`); `Unown (!)` and `Unown (?)` with a formless picture come out as plain Unown (same stats); the dev database still holds the rows of the old feed until the bot's next sync.

## From Phase 2D-1 (controls on the post, the attendance commands, 2026-10-11)

As built: commits `3b49b1c`..`c98e90b` (six tasks, each equal to the reviewed prototype) and `24789f7` (one fix wave after the whole-branch review); 1,627 unit tests, 123 database tests. The plan document shows the tasks before the fix wave; the code is the authority. Nothing of it has touched Discord: the staging checklist is at the end of the plan.

Plan 2D-2 has since brought the clock (the timeout the completion question promises) and `/hatch`; the two halves go to staging together.

**Decided by Matt, and still for him to decide:**

- Decided (Matt, 2026-10-11), for plan 2D-2: a mis-tap on Done is undone by going back. When the member who pressed Done changes to Here or Join (or otherwise is no longer done) and nobody else of the group has completed since, the question is withdrawn: its message is deleted and the members it asked are Here again.
- Decided (Matt, 2026-10-11), for plan 2D-2: private answers to clicks are gathered like the lines in the post. The first click is answered at once; further clicks by the same member on the same party within a couple of seconds are only acknowledged (kept in memory), and one final private answer a couple of seconds later says the net effect of all of them. (My reading: the later clicks are acknowledged without a message, `deferUpdate`, and the final answer is an edit of the first private answer or one follow-up; check on staging which reads better.)
- Decided (Matt, 2026-10-11): a team's emoji stands once, before the first trainer of that team in a list. Stays as built.
- Anybody, signed up or not, can open groups (four more), each pinging the attendees and becoming the default group; nothing removes an empty group. As in the legacy bot.
- Five group buttons with long labels are legal but hard to read on a phone; a shorter cut for the label on the button (the field name has it in full) is one line.

**Still open after plan 2D-2:**

- The cap of ten questions forgets one whose deletion failed.
- Component handlers still have no `defer`; a save that never settles (open since 2B) holds every click on that raid.
- The answers to the completion question share the 30-a-minute budget of the other buttons: a member who leaned on +1 cannot answer for up to a minute.

**Phase 3 (trains and meetups):** `AttendanceService.done` uses `raidStage`, `partyControls` takes a `RaidParty`, and "raid" is written into about ten texts (`PARTY_OVER`, `problemText`, the question, the help of the commands); `done` on a train means leave.

**Staging (what no fake shows; add to the checklist of the plan):** a click on a post Discord auto-archived; whether a mention that does not ping shows a name for a member the client has not loaded, and whether it adds the member to the thread; members who sign up by button do not follow the post and hear nothing until pinged; the server's own team emoji.

**Small:** the title of commit `c61baae` and one line of the plan's Review Focus still say "menu" (the groups are buttons); `NO_PROMPTS` is one shared object (never mutated, not frozen); `src/components/party.ts` imports its helpers from `src/commands/parties/reply.ts`; controls stay live on a raid that has ended by the clock until it is archived; the team emoji names are fixed (`instinct`, `mystic`, `valor`), not settings; keep the name-length test when upgrading zod (it pins that the schema counts code points).

## From Phase 2D-2 (the scheduler and the raid commands, 2026-10-11)

As built: commits `c24eba8`..`a1d2091` (nine tasks, each equal to the reviewed prototype) and `bf8cdfe` (one fix wave after the whole-branch review); 1,784 unit tests, 126 database tests. The plan document shows the tasks before the fix wave; the code is the authority. Nothing of plans 2D-1 and 2D-2 has touched Discord: the staging checklists are at the end of the two plans. Matt removed the old test posts from the test server on 2026-10-11; the "Pine Test" category with `#test-region`, `#the-bot-lab` and the forum `test-region-raids` is still there.

**Decided by Matt (2026-10-11):**

- A mis-tapped Done is taken back when the member goes back; private answers to clicks are gathered; the archive warning is repeated after every fifth message (a busy post buries it, and Discord has nothing that stays in view on a phone); a boss with only one fast or charged move has it set (Kyogre and Waterfall).
- `/location` instead of `/gym` for moving a raid is fine for now; a raid cannot move to another region until plan 2E.

**Answered by Matt (2026-10-11):**

- Anybody in the post may change any raid's times, length, boss, moves and gym: fine. Abuse is very rare, and the moderators deal with it when members report it.
- An EX or elite raid shows its date until the day itself and only the clock time from midnight on; an ordinary raid never shows a date. Done after the plan (the renderer takes the time zone; the clock's midnight render applies it).
- A correction that makes a raid an egg again puts those who were asked back to Here and leaves those who pressed Done as done: fine.
- Messages of bots and webhooks do not count towards the five that repeat the warning (other bots should not post there anyway). Done after the plan.

**Still for Matt, whenever (each is small):**

- A member who is done, or merely interested, may set the group's meeting time. `/boss` to an elite raid drops everybody's remote flag without a line in the post. `/moveset` cannot take a move away again, and offers no elite moves.
- Naming the boss of an egg without times does not hatch it: Done stays refused until somebody sets a time.

**Phase 3 (trains and meetups):** `PartyService.end(onlyIf: (party: RaidParty) => …)`, `PartyScheduler.act` (`posts.view(party).boss`), `typedPartyTime(party: RaidParty)` and `RaidChangeService.inTheWay` (`raidKindOf` over `store.active()`) are typed for raids; the `ctx.raids` commands need a `requireRaid` guard; `/meet`'s texts say "raid".

**Phase 4 (notifications):** `RaidChangeService.setBoss` drops `wasEgg` and `changed` of the domain's `setBoss`, and the tick's `bossSet` is used only for the line in the post: both are where "boss set" notifications hook in. A meeting time that was set, moved or cancelled is a message or a line in the post only.

**Phase 5:** `/admin save-party`: the clock already respects `saved`, but a party saved after its warning keeps the warning message; `settleWarning` should treat `saved` like a called-off archiving.

**Robustness, when the files are next touched:**

- Two edges of `askedBy`: a follower from an earlier round keeps a later mis-tapped Done from being taken back (Alice Done, Bob yes, Alice back, Carol arrives, Alice Done and back: Carol stays asked); and somebody who answered "Not yet" and then pressed Done themself no longer counts as having followed the first Done.
- The "Set time" form checks for existing times outside the store: two members who submit within the same milliseconds both pass.
- A bot message on its way when the party ends (a repeated warning, a question) can land after the archiving: the post is open again, still locked, until the hourly sweep. Done, Here, Done faster than one send can leave two identical questions.
- After a long downtime a group whose meeting time passed is asked and pinged at the restart, also the morning after: consider not asking when the same tick schedules the archiving.
- `/hatch` on a raid that was seen hatched accepts a time in the future; only `end` and `meet` have the extra limit. That limit takes the longest raid length over a window that reaches into the egg time after the report.
- Ends happen one after another inside a tick: after a Raid Hour some eighty raids end in the same tick, at four or five REST calls each. `openPosts` is one server-wide call per region. `end` archives also when the last edit failed, and a title that waited for the rename limit is dropped.
- After three lost races `RaidChangeService.apply` answers `gone` ("This raid is over") for a raid that is not.
- `startApp` itself has no test (it needs a token and a database); the lifecycle database test covers the same services wired by the test support. The `MessageCreate` listener has none either.
- `changingMember` and `actingMember` do the same; the tests of `AttendanceService.setMeetingTime` live in `raid-change-service.test.ts`.

**Staging (what no fake shows; in the checklist of the plan):** whether `edit({ locked: true, archived: true })` works in one call and on an already archived post; deleting a message in an archived post; whether a message sent into a locked, archived post opens it; what `editReply` does on a private answer the member dismissed; `deferUpdate` on "Yes, done" when the question is deleted at the same moment; how long the start takes with fifty posts; how the repeated warning reads on a phone.

## From Phase 2E-1 (mirrors and the last raid commands, 2026-10-11)

As built: commits `fbf9bbb`..`7915203` (eight tasks, each equal to the reviewed prototype) and `0d95288` (one fix wave after the whole-branch review); 1,888 unit tests, 126 database tests. The plan document shows the tasks before the fix wave; the code is the authority. Plan 2E was split: 2E-2 (access for members of other regions, moving a raid to another region) is next. Nothing of plans 2D-1, 2D-2 and 2E-1 has touched Discord: the staging checklists are at the end of the three plans.

**Decided or confirmed by Matt (2026-10-11):**

- `/delete` stays: it is the staff's manual clean-up for whatever the clock and the recovery do not tidy up, and it has other uses. From staff it closes the raid at once (no wait of fifteen seconds), without a throttle; from anybody else it opens a form whose reason goes into the post, one request per member and raid, the moderators pinged once per raid.
- `/where`, and `/status` in a region channel, answer privately. A raid that is over by the clock leaves the remote and EX channels at once. A shout lists only the members it pings. `/admin save-party keep:False` gives a kept post back to the clock.

**Answered by Matt (2026-10-11), done after the plan:**

- `/shout` is for members who are signed up for the raid (done or not); anybody else is refused.
- A list of kept posts is wanted for admin purposes: `/admin save-party` used outside a post, without an option, lists the kept posts of the server.
- A raid closed with `/delete` says so in the post itself: the sentence stands in the place of the times, the embed is shown as ended and the post gets the `Ended` tag.
- Bot developers get neither `/delete` nor `/admin save-party` (both are for admins, moderators and the owner); with posts archived instead of deleted there is little left to debug that way.

**Still for Matt, whenever:** a deletion request that the moderators do not act on stays in the post and is archived with it.

**Phase 3 (trains and meetups):** `PartyMirrors`, `raidMirrors` and `renderMirror` are typed and worded for raids ("A raid in …"); `/where` needs a gym (a train has a route); `requestDeletion`, the last words of `/delete` and the texts of `/shout` and `/admin save-party` say "raid".

**Phase 4 (notifications):** a shout is a message in the post only (the legacy bot had no DM either); a member who signed up through a copy is pinged in a post they may not see.

**Phase 5:** `/help` says nothing yet about what is for staff inside a command that everybody sees (`/delete`).

**Robustness, when the files are next touched:**

- Copies and the link line of a party that has ended are not tried again: a deletion that fails at the end, or a stop of the process between the store's write and the deletions, leaves them (with buttons that answer "This raid is over"). The ended row still has `mirrorMessages` and `regionLinkMessageId`, so a sweep at start could delete them.
- `PartyService.flush()` at shutdown does not wait for a `/status` on its way; its copy can end up unrecorded and undeleted.
- After a restart every copy is edited once (nothing is remembered of what it shows): N raids make N edits in one channel at Discord's pace, and an end waits for its own edit.
- `AttendanceService.shout`, `requestDeletion` and the line of `keep` check the store and then send: one that is on its way when the party ends can land after the archiving (the hourly sweep repairs it), as noted for other messages under "From Phase 2D-2".
- A party's deletion requests are sent one after the other; a send that hangs holds the later ones of that party.
- `/status` and a deletion request answer "This raid is over" when the post cannot be sent to although the party is active.
- A small leak in `PartyMirrors`' memo of what a copy shows (an edit that resolves after its copy was replaced).
- A rollback to code before this plan cannot read a row whose `mirrorMessages` has the kind `status` (nothing is deployed yet).
- The form of `/delete` shares the 30-a-minute budget of the buttons: a member who leaned on a button loses the typed reason.
- One change renders the post three times (the post, the copy in it, the shared copies); a shout can answer "I pinged 0 trainers."

**Staging (what no fake shows; in the checklist of the plan):** `messages.edit` on a copy and on the link line, also in a post Discord archived by itself; `findChannel`'s permission check with channel overwrites and with the bot's own member not cached; whether the roles `admin` and `moderator` are pinged when they are not mentionable (the bot then needs "Mention @everyone, @here and All Roles"); an ephemeral answer or follow-up in a thread that was just archived and locked; `/where` with a file in an ephemeral answer; how `<#post>` reads in the remote channel for a member who cannot see the post; Discord's real limit for edits in one channel during a busy hour.

## From Phase 2E-2 (access for members of other regions, moving a raid, 2026-10-11)

As built: commits `8fb67f6`..`d4290d0` (eight tasks, each equal to the reviewed prototype) and `76af052` (the minor findings of the whole-branch review, which found nothing critical or important); 1,970 unit tests, 131 database tests. The plan document shows the tasks before that last commit; the code is the authority. This finishes the building of Phase 2. Nothing of plans 2D-1, 2D-2, 2E-1 and 2E-2 has touched Discord: the staging checklists are at the end of the four plans, to be done in that order.

**Answered by Matt (2026-10-11), done after the plan:**

- A move to another region has the same limit as `/location` itself and none of its own: the ten changes a minute of every raid change. Anybody in the post may do it, after a yes to the question; it pings the others who are signed up (those with pings on), once, in the old post.
- A guest stays in the forum until the raid ends, also after Done: access goes when they leave, or when the raid ends or moves. (The spec had Done take it back.)
- The hard stop in the middle of a move (the raid left in both regions) is fixed in Phase 4, with `movedFrom`.

**For Matt to confirm or change (each is small):**

- A raid that is kept (`/admin save-party`) keeps its guests in the forum for as long as it is kept.
- To keep a member out of a region's forum for good, a moderator must set a permission overwrite for that member (or deny the view to a role they hold): taking the region's role away is not enough, since the bot cannot tell that from never having had it. Worth a line in the admin guide (Phase 9).
- A member overwrite takes the forum out of sync with its category for as long as it exists, so a change of the category's permissions may not reach the forum meanwhile; and "Sync permissions" by a moderator removes the bot's overwrites (the hourly sweep restores those still called for). To see on staging.
- The ended row of a moved raid says nothing of the move: history shows a raid that ended early and one that went on, with the same members.
