**Phase 3 (trains and meetups):** `relocate`, `relocatedRaid`, `carriedOver` and `gymMove` are for raids and one gym; a train or meetup needs its own rule for access (its post is in the forum of the channel it was made in) and none for moving. `AccessService` itself works on any party.

**Phase 4 (notifications):** a store listener cannot tell a moved raid from a new report (`PartyStore.add` announces both as `(undefined, party)`): the new party needs to say where it came from (`movedFrom`, a new version of the state document), which would also let a restart tidy up after a hard stop in the middle of a move, and give history the link. The members of a moved raid are told by one ping in the old post; a DM belongs here. A guest whose access to the old forum ends a moment after that ping may lose the notification: to see on staging; the DM settles it.

**Robustness, when the files are next touched:**

- A hard stop of the process after the new post of a move exists and before the old party has ended leaves the raid twice (both run out by the clock, or staff close one). Shutdown waits for a move under way.
- When somebody else ends the old party in the instant between the word "This raid has moved" and its end, that word stays in the old post although the move was taken back (the linked post is deleted).
- What is done to the new party in the second a move takes (a time, the boss, a new group) is replaced by what the old one had; only sign-ups and leaves there are kept. The carry-over is stored without the gym's lock.
- A report of the new gym during a move that then fails is answered with a post that is deleted a moment later.
- A sign-up lost to a hard stop before its grant was recorded is tried again only with the member's next step. The roles of a member who did not act themself (the others of a moved raid, a group the clock asks) are those of their last interaction: consider fetching them anew for such members.
- The bot leaving a server drops the grant rows (cascade) while the overwrites stay on Discord, and the grants in memory fail hourly until a restart.
- Every grant and take-back is an entry in the audit log and an update of the forum channel; a busy hour with many guests makes hundreds on one rate-limit bucket.
- The deletion requests to the moderators are kept by party id: after a move, members can ask again and the roles are pinged again; a request made before the move is in the archived post.
- Typed text in `/location` no longer prefers the raid's own region when a name also fits a gym across the border ("Several gyms fit"); the list offers it by name with the region.
- A second click on "Move it there" while the first is still working is told the choice has expired. A move that fails for lack of a permission in the other forum says only "Nothing was moved." and the general error. A mover who is not signed up may be given a link to a post they cannot open.
- In a forum whose permissions deny writing to everybody, a guest is let in to read only; whether slash commands in the post then work for them is to see on staging.
- `/admin setup-forums` does not check that the bot has Manage Permissions in a forum: an admin learns of it only from the log.

**Staging (what no fake shows; in the checklist of the plan):** see the checklist; beyond it, from the last review: what a `DELETE` of an overwrite that is gone answers, and a `PUT` for a member who has left; that a click after a restart makes no request for the member, and the clock asking a group of N makes N, once; how `<#post>` and `<#region>` read for somebody who cannot see them yet; `/delete` and the clock during a move held by a slow network.

## Enhancements for later (Matt, during the Phase 2 staging, 2026-10-11)

- **Merging raids.** Moving a raid (`/location`) onto a gym that already has such a raid is refused today ("That gym already has a raid like this one"). Instead it should try to merge the two where that is possible: everybody who is signed up for the raid being moved is added to the raid that is already there, in a new group of their own, and the moved raid's post is closed with a pointer to the other, as a move to another region does it. To decide when planning: what "possible" means (the same tier and boss, times that agree or the surviving raid's times win), what happens to a meeting time and to members who are in both, and whether the member is asked first. Matt (2026-10-12): it should ask how to merge instead of always making a new group: most members would probably choose to join the existing main group, so that is the first choice offered, with "as a new group" beside it.

- **A pass over the look of the posts (Matt, 2026-10-11).** Once all functionality is built and verified as working, towards the end of the implementation and before cutover: make the status posts (the embed and its buttons, for raids, meetups and trains, and their copies) more pleasing to look at. Matt found in the Phase 2 staging that the legacy bot's status messages with their emoji "buttons" looked nicer than the new posts in the raid channels. To do with Matt in front of Discord, comparing with the legacy bot's messages: the embed's layout and wording (fields against running text, the legacy field titles, team and status emoji, the thumbnail), the buttons (emoji on them, labels, colours, how many rows), and what the server's custom emoji (`settings.emoji`) can do there. It is a change of the renderers (`src/render/`) only; nothing before it should polish the look for its own sake.

- **A review of every command name (Matt, 2026-10-12).** Slash commands have no aliases, and the legacy bot had many, some used more than the command's own name (`!wild` rather than `!spawn`). Before cutover, give Matt one table of every slash command with the legacy command and all its aliases beside it, so he can pick the names members will look for; a second name is possible where it is worth it (the same `CommandModule` registered under two names: it shows twice in Discord's list and counts toward the limit of 100 commands). Decided already: the spawn report is `/wild` (plan 4B).

- **A default count, and a member of two teams (Matt, 2026-10-12; shelved).** The legacy bot had neither. A `/settings` entry for the number of people a member usually brings would pre-fill the count at sign-up. A member who plays two accounts on different teams could hold two team roles, but the bot takes one team per member (`teamOfRoles`: the first of instinct, mystic, valor) and a post lists them under that one; showing both needs the sign-up to say which of their accounts are of which team. Until this is taken up, teams are a pick-one group in the role menu of plan 4C.

## From the Phase 2 staging run (2026-10-11, first part: one account)

Run on the test server with the dev application, the bot started by Matt, Discord driven from his owner account in the built-in browser. The notes of the run are in the git-ignored `var/stage/results.md`; the settings copy with a fast clock (`var/stage/settings.json`), the log reader (`var/stage/log.mjs`) and the script for the second region (`var/stage/db2.mts`) are beside it. The test server now has `remote-raids`, `potential-ex-gym-raids`, `test-region-east` and its forum `test-region-east-raids`; the local database has region 2 (the invented east outline) with two gyms, and gym 2 has the EX tag.

**Passed:** everything of the 2D-1 and 2D-2 checklists that one account can do (buttons, attendance commands, groups, "Set time", the hatch, the raid changes, the warning and its repeat after five messages, archiving and locking); a restart (clean shutdown, catching up on a raid that ended meanwhile, the sweep archiving an open post of an ended party, old posts shown anew with working buttons, the access grants loaded); of 2E-1 the copies in both channels, Join on a copy, the link line and copies following a boss change, `/status`, `/where`, `/shout`, `/admin save-party`, the staff `/delete`; of 2E-2 the move to another region (question, Cancel, the move, old and new post, link lines, copies) and the refusal of a move onto a taken gym. What the fakes could not show and now is known: `edit({ locked, archived })` works in one call; a private answer stays readable in a locked, archived post; `<#region>` and `<#post>` render as links in a message; Discord's client lets only one click through while a button's interaction is pending, so bursts of one member are rare.

**Fixed after the run (2026-10-11, before Phase 3 was planned):**

- An egg says what it hatches as once: the first line is just "T5 egg" and the block under it is headed "Hatches as Yveltal" (Matt). Only an egg whose boss the game master does not know, which has no block, keeps ", which hatches as …" in the first line.
- An egg whose boss a member named is shown the same way until it hatches (not "T5 raid against Yveltal"), and its moves line appears only once a move is known. The post's title still names the boss.
- The link line in the region channel is "New raid: #post": Discord shows the post's current title in the link, so the line is no longer edited when the title changes (the code that followed the title is gone).
- "It hatched at … and ends at …" (and "ended") for times that are past; `/meet` says "I have told the others." only when somebody else is in the group; the range of a refused time names clock times, and dates only when an end of the range is more than half a day away.
- A move to another region onto a gym that has such a raid is refused before the question is asked (the service still decides again at the move).
- The `gym` option of `/raid` and `/location` with nothing typed offers the region's own gyms by name (`GymProvider.inRegion`).

**Left as it is:** Discord writes its own "changed the post title" notice into the post at every rename (hatch, `/location`). Nothing is done about it unless Matt minds; a bot can delete such a system message.

None of these fixes has been seen on Discord yet; they are looked at with the second-account staging.

**Done during the run:** a gym is now looked for in the other regions of the server when no gym of the region or next to it has every typed word (`gymHits` in `raid-input.ts`, commit `e0001bf`; Matt). Not yet seen on Discord: the running bot was started before it.

**Still to do with Matt, with a second account that is not owner, admin or moderator:** the pings (mentions shown as names, the new-group notice, `/meet`, `/shout`), the completion question between two members and its timeout, the `/delete` request form with its role ping, the whole "Access" list of the 2E-2 checklist, what the members of a moved raid see. With one account, skipped for time: permissions taken from the bot in `remote-raids`, a copy deleted by hand, `/admin save-party` on a warned raid, several moves in a row, the team emoji (a role named `mystic`, `valor` or `instinct`), the run with ten raids for the rate limits.

## From Phase 3A (party kinds and meetups, 2026-10-11)

Plan `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-10-11-phase-3a-party-kinds-meetups.md`, executed and complete at `54bf512`: 2,268 unit and 135 database tests. Prototyped on a worktree by an Opus agent from a design brief, reviewed by two Opus reviewers and re-reviewed after the fixes, transcribed (the implementer applied the plan's blocks by script; the branch equalled the prototype), then the whole-branch review with one fix wave. The legacy behaviour of trains and meetups is digested in the git-ignored `.superpowers/sdd/phase-3-legacy-notes.md`; the reviews' findings and rulings are in `.superpowers/sdd/2026-10-11-phase-3a-party-kinds-meetups/`.

**Decided without Matt beyond the four rulings he approved (say so if any should be different):**

- How a time typed for a meetup is read leaves the legacy rule, which read times into the past: never into the past; on the day of the start or end while that day has not passed, otherwise today; `6:15` for a start is 18:15; an end without a date falls on the next day only within twelve hours of the start, otherwise it is refused with the form that names the date. A bare number is an hour (`/meet time:20` is 20:00) and minutes need `in`, as Phase 1A ported it.
- The day after which a meetup without times counts as over runs from when it was made or from when its last time was removed, whichever is later (refines "one day after creation").
- A meetup always names its maker ("Created by"), whatever their raid privacy says; the first archive warning of a meetup without an end time pings the maker when they are still signed up and allow mentions.
- `/meet` and `/cancel-meet` in a meetup are limited to 3 per 180 seconds (a new start pings everybody who is signed up), and the announcement of a new start is gathered for five seconds.
- A running meetup is blue; times that are not today are shown with their weekday.

**For plan 3B (trains):**

- A train is `timed` + `train` in the union and in the state document (a new version, 5). `TimedParty` becomes a union: `TimedPartyService.setDescription`, `commandTimed`'s and `infoControls`' "This meetup is over.", `describeTimes` in `meetup-post.ts` (timed-party-shaped, lives in the meetup renderer), the last branch of `partyFromStored`, and `A_TIMED_PARTY` in `guards.ts` are the places that say "meetup" for "timed party".
- A train has groups and a boss: `isRaidAction` (`render/party-controls.ts`) answers "This raid is over." for `done` and `group`; everything about a boss is typed for a raid (`requireRaid` on `/boss`, `RaidChangeService.setBoss`, `shownBoss`, `RaidDetails`), so "a party with a boss" is a generalisation 3B must make.
- `summaryText` (`party-service.ts`) lists raids and meetups by kind: a train would be missing. `requireGym` in `status.ts` (`/where`) would tell a train it is at no gym (a train's gym is its current stop). The archive warning pings the maker; a train has a conductor.
- The third copy of the `apply` skeleton (`AttendanceService`, `RaidChangeService`, `TimedPartyService`) should become one before a fourth is written.
- The gathered announcement exists only for a party-wide start; a raid group's `announceMeeting` is still sent at once, unordered.

**Open, small:**

- A post deleted by hand is noticed only at the next render (a change, the start, the end, midnight): the party stays active and the region's list links to nothing until then. `ThreadDelete` (the `Guilds` intent delivers it) or a render of every party with the hourly upkeep.
- Two members with `/info` open at once: the later wins silently.
- `/meetup` could take optional `start` and `end` options.
- The pinned list is still titled "Active raids" and says "No raids right now" above a list of meetups; renaming needs the existing pinned posts renamed.
- The help categories: the attendance commands stay under "Raid Basics" and `/end` under "Raid Creation and Maintenance" although they serve meetups.
- A name of characters nobody can see that are no control or format characters (U+3164, U+2800, a lone joiner) is accepted.
- A refused `/meet` with a start after the end, or one that changes nothing, counts against its limit.
- A kept meetup older than 93 days can no longer get a start (the range counts from creation), and the refusal then shows a reversed range.
- `/meet 10.17` is read as 22:17 (the dot separates hours and minutes); `noon`, `midnight`, `tomorrow 6pm` are not read.
- A start announcement waiting its five seconds is lost on a crash (a clean shutdown sends it).
- A name or text that a server's AutoMod blocks gives the generic error, and every later render of that post fails (group labels and shouts have the same exposure).
- `escapeMarkdown` of discord.js leaves `**` inside long runs of `*`; raids have the same.
- The base branch cannot read a database this branch has written to (state document version 4), as with every version.

**Staging:** the checklist at the end of the plan, after the open second-account checks of Phase 2. What only Discord can show, from the final review: how a broken masked link renders in an embed description; the `/info` form (prefilled text with line breaks, an empty submission, how Discord counts emoji against the 1,000); a title with raw Markdown or right-to-left text; the `Meetup` tag on forums made before this plan (`/admin setup-forums` adds it); `Ended` set at the end time and again at the close; the weekday form of times and the re-render at midnight.

## From Phase 3B (raid trains, 2026-10-12)

Plan `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-10-11-phase-3b-trains.md`, executed and complete at `15bf5c8`: 2,580 unit and 150 database tests. Built like 3A: a design brief with rulings (`.superpowers/sdd/phase-3b-design.md`), an Opus prototype on a worktree, two Opus reviewers, two fix rounds with a re-review between them, the plan generated from the prototype, one Sonnet implementer (the branch equalled the prototype), the whole-branch review and one fix wave. Findings and rulings are in `.superpowers/sdd/2026-10-11-phase-3b-trains/`.

**Decided by Matt:** the movement buttons are a private panel behind one "Move train…" button, apart from the attendance buttons (2026-10-11). Approved afterwards (2026-10-12): a route edit that moves the train counts as a movement; the announcement goes by gym; Finish leaves Here as Here; `/boss` in a train ignores a tier and accepts any known Pokémon; a panel button remembers the stop it was drawn at; "Move train…" on a train without a route answers with a sentence.

**Decided without Matt (say so if any should be different):**

- Anybody who is signed up may edit the route and take over as conductor; staff (admin, moderator, owner) may always move a train and edit its route without being signed up; `/route clear` is for bot management and the conductor; `/conductor clear` for the conductor and staff.
- A gym of another region of the server is added to a route without a question; a gym twice and more than 50 stops are refused.
- The first stop of an empty route pings nobody and needs no right to move. `/route load` always needs the right to move.
- Finishing a route ends nothing and changes no time: a finished train without an end time stays until its fallback day. (The alternative: it counts as over from when it finished.)
- Earlier movement announcements stay in the post (the legacy bot kept only the last).
- `/route save` works for anybody in the post; the same name without case replaces; at most 25 per member and region; `/route forget` is new. `/boss none` takes a train's boss off.
- `/route show` sends a long route as several private messages.

**Decided by Matt (2026-10-12), and built as plan 3C the same day ("From Phase 3C" below):**

- **A train reaches the regions its route touches.** A member who cannot see the forum of the train's region has no control to sign up with. A bare link line would not do: `<#post>` shows as an unknown channel to somebody who cannot see the forum. What works is what a raid has in the remote and EX channels: a copy of the train's message with its buttons (Join on a copy signs the member up, and `AccessService` then lets them into the forum), in the region channel of every other region the route has a stop in, kept by `PartyMirrors` (a new kind of copy per region channel; added and removed as the route changes, deleted at the end). To settle when planning: whether "Move train…" belongs on such a copy, and what the copy says above the embed.
- **A mistaken Finish can be taken back.** Previous on a finished route leads back to the stop the train was at when it was finished, not to the last stop. The train remembers that stop (a new field, so state document version 6); a route edit after the finish keeps it on its gym or forgets it.

**Open, small:**

- A raid group's meeting announcement is still sent at once, not gathered (it would change what a raid does).
- A move taken back within the window is rightly not announced, but who was Here is left as Coming, and the mover has read "I am telling the others."
- A finished train that gets a loaded route starting at a gym of the old route is announced as "moving back to".
- `/route load` replaces the whole route for anybody who may move the train, while `/route clear` is for staff and the conductor: without a conductor a one-stop saved route gets round it.
- "This has ended." where a raid member used to read "This raid is over.": a group button of an ended raid, and three races between a guard and its handler.
- `/name`, `/meet` and `/end` are not under "Trains" in `/help`; the "Raid Basics" field of `/help` is at about 990 of 1,024 characters, and a little more text there turns the field into names only (nothing tests it with the real command list).
- With many trains and many meetups the raids are left 800 characters of the region's list. `fitRoute` near the end of a long route shows fewer stops than would fit.
- A stop number picked from the autocomplete is stale when somebody edits the route before the command is sent (the answer names the gym).
- Saved-route names are compared in code, not by the column's collation ("Café" and "Cafe" are two routes); the list reads the database on every keystroke.
- A movement announcement waiting its five seconds is lost on a crash; one sent just as the post is archived opens it again until the hourly sweep. One test of `gathered.test.ts` uses real timers.
- In a train's `/boss`, a partial name fits only current bosses (`bulba` is refused though the list offers Bulbasaur); `mega pikachu` sets Pikachu.

**For later phases:**

- **Phase 4 (notifications):** the hook for the new-train DM belongs after `createTracked` answers `created` in `PartyService.createTimed` (`store.onChange` fires when the post does not exist yet). `/settings mentions-train-stops` writes what the movement ping already reads. An announcement reads the settings of each attendee one by one: a batch read is due.
- **Phase 5 (gym administration):** a deleted gym stays in the routes of active trains ("Unknown gym" as a stop, also in the announcement) and in saved routes; an edited gym (name, notice, EX) shows in a train's post only at its next change. Decide whether deleting a gym edits routes, and render train posts anew after `refreshGym`.
- **Phase 8 (import of legacy trains):** the document refuses a gym twice and more than 50 stops, and legacy routes can have both (ids as numbers and as text, `{gymId}` objects): the import must dedupe and cut while keeping the train at its gym; `currentGym = length + 1` maps to `route.length`; the conductor is a user object and need not be signed up (sign them up or drop them); Complete has no meaning in a train; `gymId` must be null. Legacy saved routes may share a name within a member and region (the list then shows two identical choices) and a member may have more than 25.

**Staging:** the checklist at the end of the plan, after 3A's. What only Discord can show, from the reviews: whether Discord accepts an integer option with a minimum and autocomplete, and whether words can be typed into it; `update` on a private panel, a press after fifteen minutes, a press on a panel whose post was archived (does it open the post again); masked links `[name](<url>)` in a message and in the embed, `1\.` in a list; mentions in the embed and "Moved by" pinging nobody; several stops stepped through quickly in a busy post; the post with twenty stops on a phone.

## From Phase 3C (a train in the regions it stops in, a Finish taken back, 2026-10-12)

Plan `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-10-12-phase-3c-train-copies-finish.md`, both things Matt asked for after 3B, executed and complete at `eb1fd3e`: 2,634 unit and 150 database tests. Built like 3B (brief with rulings in `.superpowers/sdd/phase-3c-design.md`, an Opus prototype, two reviewers, two fix rounds with a re-review, one Sonnet implementer, the whole-branch review and a small fix wave); findings and rulings are in `.superpowers/sdd/2026-10-12-phase-3c-train-copies-finish/`.

**Decided without Matt (say so if any should be different):**

- A copy has the post's buttons without "Move train…": it is an invitation, and the train is moved from its post.
- Copies stand for the whole route (also the stops behind the train, also once the route is finished) until the train ends, in every region a stop lies in (a gym where regions overlap counts for all of them).
- The line above a copy: "A raid train that stops in this region. Sign up here to follow it in its post: <#post>". To a member who cannot see the forum yet, Discord shows that link as an unknown channel until they have signed up.
- A region channel where the bot may not post embeds gets no copy (Discord would show the message without its embed, not refuse it).
- Going back to the stop a train was finished from leaves who is Here as Here; the panel's Previous button now carries where it leads, so a stale one is refused ("That button no longer leads where it said.").
- A copy in a shared channel whose edit or send failed is tried again ten minutes later without waiting for the party to change. This also holds for a raid's copies in the remote and EX channels.

**Open, small:**

- The train is not in the pinned list of the other regions' forums, and a member who signed up on a copy is not told where the post is once they were let in (the link above the copy starts working).
- A copy stays where it was first sent in a chat channel and scrolls away; members there are not told when the train starts or moves. A moderator cannot keep a copy out of a channel: one deleted by hand comes back with the train's next change.
- Copies left when their deletion fails at the end of a party (also a copy in a channel the bot can no longer view), and a copy sent just before a crash that was not recorded yet: a sweep at start over the `mirrorMessages` of ended rows would close both (the item of "From Phase 2E-1" weighs more now that a copy can stand in another region's chat). Nothing brings copies up to date after a restart either (the retry is in memory).
- A region whose channel was deleted while its row stays: every train that stops there tries a send and logs a warning every ten minutes.
- Members of several regions signing up for one train all get an overwrite on one forum, so the limit per channel is reached sooner than with raids.
- A change of a train is one edit per region copy, one after the other; one rate-limited channel holds up the others of that run.
- `SHARED_KINDS` and the `status` path are two lists in `PartyMirrors`: a new kind in neither is never synced.
- Previous, Finish, Previous within the window is announced as "moving to", not "moving back to". Two members who both type `/previous` to undo a Finish move the train two stops back (the panel is safe). The post itself does not say that Previous takes a Finish back; "I am telling the others." is also said for a Finish taken back within five seconds, when nothing is then told.
- The retry uses a raw `setTimeout` (the party service has an injectable timer); two tests wait 20 ms of real time before asserting "not archived yet".

**For later phases:**

- **Phase 4 (notifications):** a member who signed up on a copy is let into the forum on the side, and a barred one never is: a ping or DM that links to the post can arrive before they can see it, or for a post they never will.
- **Phase 5 (gym and region administration):** a deleted gym must come off a route through `removeStop` or a new function built on `withRoute`, never by filtering `train.route` (that can leave `finishedFrom` at a gym that is gone, and the store then refuses every write of that train, also its end). After `GymDirectory.refreshGym` / `load()` or a region change, ask for `changed` on the active trains: their copies follow the regions of their gyms only at their next change, and the two directories are reloaded separately.
- **Phase 8 (import of legacy trains):** imported trains need `finishedFrom: null`, also one that is past its last stop; a train with stops in other regions sends its copies at its first render (a burst per region channel).
- **Speed of the check (Matt asked, 2026-10-12):** of the unit suite's time, running the tests is about 72%, imports 24%, the TypeScript transform 4%; lint takes 35 s, typecheck 11 s, the format check 10 s. Profiling the slowest test files (real `setImmediate` turns, polling `vi.waitFor`) is the lever, if wanted.

**Staging:** the checklist at the end of the plan, after 3A's and 3B's; it needs a second region with a channel and a gym on the test server, and the second account (active again since 2026-10-12) that cannot see the forum of the train's region. From the final review, in addition: taking Embed Links away while a copy stands deletes it at the next change (this also confirms on the real API that the embed is dropped, not refused); View Channel off for the bot (the deletion fails with one log line, the copy stays); a region channel deleted while a train stops there; a panel opened before the deploy (its Previous is refused once, the fresh panel works); a train made before this plan (a version 5 row) loads, moves, finishes and is written back; `/help previous` and `/help finish` at their real width.

## From the Phase 3 staging run (2026-10-12, first part)

Run on the test server with the dev application (commands registered and the bot started by Matt; Discord driven from his owner account in the built-in browser, the second ordinary account "Tester" by Matt). The notes are in the git-ignored `var/stage/results.md`. The permission classifier refuses Claude every permission edit in Discord and the command registration: both are Matt's.

**Passed:** of 3A, `/meetup` with its post, Join, `/meet` with the gathered announcement, `/end`, `/name`, the `/info` form, `/status`, the refusals in a meetup's words, and the clock with an end time (Ended, closed and locked). Of 3B, `/train`, `/route add` (the region's gyms offered for an empty option, a gym of the other region, a gym twice refused), `/route remove`, `/route save`, `/route show`, the move panel (the disabled Previous at the first stop is accepted by Discord, Next edits the panel in place, a stale panel is refused and drawn anew), `/next`, `/conductor set`, `/boss` with a Pokémon that is no current boss, `/where`, the movement message pinging the other member and not the mover, `/delete` by staff. Of 3C, the copy in the other region's channel within seconds, following the post, deleted with the region's last stop and at the end of the train; Finish from a middle stop and Previous back to it ("moving back to"), nothing said when both happen within five seconds, the remembered stop surviving a restart, one copy only after a restart. With the second account: the link above the copy shows as an unknown channel until the member signs up on the copy, then works without a reload; the member is let into the forum at once, can write in the post, is refused "Move train…" while there is a conductor, and loses the forum within a second or two of Leave.

**Decided by Matt during the run, and fixed the same day:** a member's own entry on a forum keeps them out only by what it effectively does. Discord's "reset to default" leaves an empty entry behind, which the bot read as "kept out on purpose" (logged, but nothing tells a moderator). An empty overwrite of the member's own now counts as none (`memberAccess`); one that sets anything is still left alone. Not yet seen on Discord. **Widened in the second part of the run (same day):** the "reset" entry of a member who had been added to a private channel was not empty, it still allowed Connect (Discord adds a member to a private channel with View and Connect, and a forum's settings do not show Connect), so the bot still read it as kept out. Now only an entry of the member's own that denies something keeps them out; one that only allows counts as none and is replaced by the bot's (what it allowed is gone when the bot takes its own entry back: accepted).

**Findings, open:**

- **Decided by Matt (2026-10-12) and built the same day (not yet seen on Discord): name plus mention, to try.** As built: `memberText` / `memberName` in `src/render/text.ts`; the name of the member who made a party is kept with the party (`makerName`, state document version 7) so that "Reported by" and "Created by" have it also when they are not signed up; who is named in a line is the acting member with the name of that interaction, and anybody else (a conductor who is replaced) by what the party has. A member the bot never saw act (the maker of a party made before this, until they act on it) is still a mention. What was decided: A line that names a member showed as "@unknown-user is coming." in a client that has not loaded that member: the bot's messages ping nobody, so Discord sends no member data with them, and the bot cannot know what a viewer's client has loaded. So wherever the bot names a member without pinging them in a line of a post ("is coming", "Moved by", who set a time or a boss, who closed a party), it writes the stored display name in bold and the mention after it: "**Tester** (@Tester) is here". The trainer lists, the maker and the conductor in the embed show the stored name only (doubling every entry is too long). Messages that are meant to ping keep their real mentions. The name is that of the member's last interaction (`noteMember`); a member the bot has no name for keeps the bare mention. To look at in the next staging run and with Matt: whether it reads well.
- `/meet time:in 1` set a start 23 seconds ahead: the minute is cut, not counted from now. The legacy bot added the minutes to the exact time and kept the seconds (shown as the same minute, but up to 59 seconds later). **Decided by Matt and fixed (2026-10-12):** a duration is rounded up to the next whole minute (`resolvePartyTime`), so "in 1" is at least a full minute away; not yet seen on Discord.
- A masked link in a meetup's information is broken as intended, but the bare address inside it is still made a link by Discord. Left as it is (Matt agreed): a visible address shows where it leads.
- Right after a page load the owner's client showed the link above a copy as "unknown" although the owner can see the forum (the client's cache; it was a link before and after).

**Second part (2026-10-12, afternoon), passed:** with the second account, the completion question between two members (asked with a ping, "Not yet" deletes it, unanswered it ends with "did not answer and counts as done" after the configured minute), the `/delete` request of an ordinary member (the form, the request with the reason and the role ping in the post), the pings of `/meet`, `/shout` and a new group, and a raid moved to the other region and back (the old post names the new one and pings, access to the old forum is taken back within a second, access to the new one given: this is where the widened access rule above was found and confirmed). Of 3B: `/route load`, `move`, `save`, `forget`, `clear`, Skip on the panel, the conductor leaving, groups, `/status` in a train, and the clock with an end time (the warning, then closed). One "interaction failed" flashed on the second account's client for a click the bot answered in 230 ms; not seen again.

**Third part (same afternoon), passed:** a kept meetup (`/admin save-party`: the post says so, and it stays open after its end, with the Ended tag and no warning), `/delete` of a meetup by staff, the throttle of `/train`, the remembered stop removed after a Finish (the stale Previous answers "That button no longer leads where it said." and the panel is drawn anew), two trains through one region (a copy of each in the other region's channel), a copy deleted by hand (sent anew at the train's next change), and Embed Links denied to the bot in the other region's channel (the copy is deleted at the train's next change and nothing is sent without its embed; the copy of a train that does not change stays until it does).

**Finding, looked at and left:** after a fresh load of the Discord client, a link to a post (`New raid: <#post>` in the region channel, the link above a train's copy) reads "#unknown", for the owner too, until the post or its forum has been opened; clicking it opens the post all the same. Seen only in the web client of the built-in browser, where it stays so for at least half a minute. Matt checked the same day: the phone app shows "unknown" for a split second and then the names, and the desktop app shows the names after a reload (second account, signed up for a train of that forum). Nothing to change; kept here in case members on the web client report it.

**Not run yet:** of 3A, the clock without times (it takes a day) and a form sent after the close. Of 3B, a panel older than fifteen minutes; twenty stops cannot be made on the test data (ten gyms). Of 3C, View Channel taken from the bot; the test regions do not overlap. With the second account, the mention settings.

## From Phase 4A (member settings, wants, gym targets, friend codes, 2026-10-12)

Plan 4A is complete (`2026-10-12-phase-4a-member-settings-wants.md`; seven tasks and a closing fix wave; four reviews of the prototype and a whole-branch review, none with a Critical finding). Nothing of it has touched Discord: its staging checklist is at the end of the plan.

**Decided by Matt on 2026-10-12, for plans 4B and 4C:**

- **Mirror the legacy bot wherever nothing speaks against it.** A meeting time set or cancelled is a DM to the other members of the group (not the one who set it, not those who are done), with no opt-out, in the legacy words ("<name> set a meeting time of <time> for <post>. There are currently **N** trainers attending!"); the ping in the post stays.
- **Sending.** One bot token. `DmSender` is an interface over a queue on the main thread with a cap (about ten requests a second) and priorities (a meeting time before wants); the message is built once per event, recipients are filtered in batches that yield, and queue depth, delivery time and event-loop delay (`monitorEventLoopDelay`) are logged. The legacy bot once lost its gateway connection when a popular want was sent on the main thread: that must not be possible. A second token with a worker thread only if the measurements call for it (its "Stop these" button would send its click to the second application). A real load test needs many accounts and is not practical: the queue is tested with a fake, and the first busy day after cutover gives the numbers.
- **The spawn report is `/wild`** (slash commands have no aliases, and `!wild` was the name in use).
- **Unown** is an ordinary species: who wants it gets the DM, and where the server has the sightings channel (`settings.channels.unown`) the report is also posted there. The bot no longer assigns or pings the `unown` role and does not tie the channel's permissions to the want. A member whose interaction shows the `unown` role and who has no Unown want in that server gets a spawn want for Unown, once per member and server (remembered, so that removing it sticks); see Phase 8.
- **A shadow want is for any Pokémon**, current boss or not; only current ones can fire.
- **The auto-assigned role is not built** (4C is `/iam`, `/iamnot`, `/admin self-roles`): Discord's own onboarding does it, and production has no Server Members intent.

- **For 4C (Matt, 2026-10-12, while 4B was prototyped):** `/iam` without a role should be a menu in which a member ticks and unticks roles (their own preselected), not only a list; servers have more than 25 self-assignable roles, so the plan must work that out (proposed: several menus in one private message, 25 roles each in alphabetical ranges, four menus and a row of page buttons beyond 125 roles). Autocomplete on `/iam role` and `/iamnot role` stays. Matt: the roles fall into logical groups (regions, teams and so on), which the legacy tables do not record. So 4C adds groups (additive: a group per role, with a name, an order and whether only one of its roles may be held, as for teams), `/admin self-roles` sets them, one menu per group (a group of more than 25 roles is split into ranges), and roles without a group share a last menu. At cutover every existing role is without a group until an admin sorts them. Confirmed by Matt: regions are multi-select; teams are single-select with "None" as a choice (no team role). Sorting the roles into groups is a one-time step for admins when they move to the new bot (it belongs in the Phase 8 migration guide), repeated only when regions are added or deleted (not when borders change): the Phase 5 commands that add or delete a region should remind the admin of the region's role.

**What 4B needs that 4A does not have** (from the whole-branch review; nothing forces a rewrite):

- A read by event on both storages: the members (snowflakes, so a join to `User`) with a want row for these numbers in this server, and with a gym target for this gym. The indexes `(pokemon, guildId)` and `(gym, guildId)` exist; compare `gym` with a string. The rows must go through `readWant` with the form reader, never be matched by number (a query for -6 also returns the perfect-IV rows of 2019). A helper for "the code of this tier" (today only `encodeNotification(...).pokemon`, which throws for a tier without one).
- Members in batches, with what they can see: `MemberDirectory.find` is one REST call per member and keeps 1,000 answers, and `ServerMember` drops the roles. Look into the gateway request for members by user id (100 per request; check that it needs no privileged intent) and decide which member cache the visibility check trusts (discord.js's own, which `memberAccess` reads, or the directory's).
- A spelling of a want for the "Stop these" button: `wantToken` has `:` and empty parts, which `encodeCustomId` refuses; the id must also carry the server (the button is pressed in a DM) and a form key may be 64 characters, so with kind, id, action and a guild snowflake it can pass 100. Either a second spelling or the token kept in a store. (This also settles the old item "the spec's `dm:stop:pokemon:150` does not fit the custom-id codec".)
- Who has `newTrain` on: the legacy bot read every `User` row with `newTrain = 1`, without a server; `UserSettingsStore` has no such read.
- One DM per member and event: a member can match by a species row, a form row, a shadow row, a tier row and a gym target at once; the DM must say which want fired, for the button.
- Decisions the codec leaves open: a `-5` row fires for ordinary tier 5 only (Matt, 2026-10-10); what an EX want fires for (`/want ex` promises "an EX raid or egg"; the legacy bot notified nobody of EX raids; see the convention on EX raids in `CLAUDE.md`).
- The sentences of 4A promise: "in a region you can see" (a visibility check per recipient) and "raid or egg" for a tier.
- `/settings boss-set` and `new-train` are offered where `features.notifications` is off and say so; if 4B gates those DMs on the feature the sentence is right, otherwise change it.

**Left open:**

- `Unown (!)` and `Unown (?)` typed into `/want` are read as every form of Unown (the tokeniser drops the sign); the keys and `exclamation point` / `question mark` read as the form.
- "Shadow X" is offered for species that cannot be shadow (`PokemonEntry.shadow` could filter; left so that a boss newer than the game master is never hidden).
- `/target` autocomplete makes a `Guild` query per keystroke, `/unwant` and `/untarget` read the member's rows per keystroke: to watch on staging.
- `MAX_CHOICES` has a third copy in `src/commands/util/help.ts`. The pure functions of gym targets live in the repository file, those of wants in `src/domain/`.
- `/nickname` may be read as the Discord server nickname, and the option of `/want` is called `target` beside the command `/target`: for the review of command names.
- A legacy `(-6, both)` perfect-IV row keeps firing for Mega Legendary raids in the legacy bot while it runs (its own collision; it ends at cutover).
- `npm run db:codegen -- --verify` on this machine (see the gotcha in `CLAUDE.md`).

**While both bots run on one database, and for a rollback:** the legacy bot ignores `PokemonNotification.form` and `shadow` and treats such a row as a want for the whole species. Its `!want <pokemon>` finds the member's first row with that number, whatever its form or shadow flag, and changes its type and server; its `!unwant <pokemon>` deletes every row of that number in the server (species, forms, shadow). A rollback needs nothing for the two columns (the legacy bot never names them), beyond what is already listed for the new tier codes.

**Phase 5 (admin):** `/admin member-settings member` can reuse `settingsLines` (`src/commands/settings/settings.ts`) and the counts of `ctx.wants` and `ctx.gymTargets`.

## From Phase 4B (delivery: DMs, stop buttons, `/wild`, Unown, 2026-10-13)

Plan 4B is complete (`2026-10-13-phase-4b-delivery.md`; ten tasks and a closing fix wave; four reviews of the prototype and a whole-branch review, none with a Critical finding). Nothing of it has touched Discord: its staging checklist is at the end of the plan, and it needs the second account for nearly everything.

**Decided by Matt while it was built (2026-10-12 and 13):** three priorities of sending (meeting times and boss-set; spawns; raid wants, gym targets, new trains); every spawn report has a picture, from the list of the game's icon files, and raid and train posts use it where the feed has none; new-train DMs are per server; a boss-set member is told every boss a raid is set to; boss-set stays one setting for all servers.

**Decided by the controller while Matt slept:**

- `/wild` allows five reports in half an hour per member (Matt, 2026-10-13, in place of the legacy 15 in a quarter of an hour, with which one member could DM every wanter of a species that often). Every report is told: the controller's first rule, one round of DMs per reporter and Pokémon in half an hour, was overruled by Matt (a rare Pokémon does spawn several times in different places).
- Text a member typed for `/wild`, and a member's name in a DM or a spawn report, cannot carry a working link (`plainNoLinks`); a meetup's description still can. Confirmed by Matt: a clickable link there would be a security risk.
- Not yet seen by Matt: a form that only changes the look (Scatterbug's patterns) shows the species' plain picture; a species whose pictures exist per form only shows a preferred form (Zygarde 50%, Basculin red-striped, Vivillon meadow and so on), never a Mega or Gigantamax picture.

**What only staging can show** (beside the plan's checklist):

- **The gateway's request for members by user id without a privileged intent.** Everything rests on Discord's documentation and the discord.js source. Watch the first raid somebody else wants: an answer for a member, a non-member and a member who cannot see the forum; the log lines "failed … one by one" and "works again"; the shard's close codes. If Discord refuses, the REST fallback carries it (one request per member; production's most wanted Pokémon has 245 members).
- **Error 40003** (opening DM channels too fast) on the first burst to members without an open DM channel: the sender then pauses a minute, for all DMs. Read the burst line ("direct messages sent": sent, closed, failed, delivery median and worst, event-loop delay). These numbers decide about a second token; the first busy day after cutover gives the real ones.
- In a DM: whether the address of a forum post opens the post on desktop and on a phone, how `<#region>` shows to somebody whose client has not loaded the channel, the picture from Leek Duck's CDN, the reasons line in small print.
- The icon list from GitHub's API from the production host (unauthenticated, 60 requests an hour per address), and that Leek Duck's CDN serves every file the list names (the list is PokeMiners', the files are Leek Duck's).
- A stop of the bot with messages still in line and with look-ups in flight; a stop button pressed after a restart.
- Migration `2026_10_12_006` on a copy of production (three users with new-train on, one real server).
- `DiscordDmGateway` (the remembered DM channel per member) and the fresh member fetch in `admitMember` are Discord code without unit tests.

**Left open:**

- A cap on the DMs one member can cause per hour, whatever the command (`/boss` cycling through the bosses tells each boss's wanters once per raid, and a new `/raid` is a new memory; the bounds today are the commands' own throttles).
- `/wild` from a post by a member who is in that forum only through an access grant: the report goes into a region text channel they cannot see, and the link in their answer is dead.
- The memory of what was told is in memory: after a restart a boss change may repeat a DM.
- The first picture of a species with per-form files is by a short table; servers may want other choices.
- Each retry of a failed game-data refresh downloads the 1 MB icon listing again; fine while `retryMs` is above a minute.
- `wanting()` returns every member with a want for the numbers in one query (production: 245 at most); a far larger server would want it in pages.
- The settings key `gameData.iconsUrl` is required: an existing `data/settings.json` of another installation needs it added at the upgrade (for the Phase 8 migration guide).
- discord.js looks for a member's DM channel by scanning all cached channels; the bot keeps its own map of DM channel ids to avoid that after the first message. To watch with many thousands of DM channels.

**While both bots run on one database, and for a rollback:** the legacy bot knows nothing of `NewTrainNotification` and `UnownWantGift` and is not disturbed by them. Migration 006 copies `User.newTrain` once, at the first start of the new bot; what members change afterwards with `/settings new-train` is not written back to the legacy column, so a rollback loses those changes (three users in production have the flag). Both tables cascade away when the bot leaves a server; after a new invitation, holders of the Unown role who had removed their want are given it again.

**Phase 8:** the Unown item of the cutover list is done by this plan (the want is given at a role holder's first interaction). The admin migration guide gets: add `gameData.iconsUrl`; the `unown` role can be deleted once members have interacted, or kept for the channel's permissions (the bot no longer uses it for anything else).

## From Phase 4C (self-assigned roles, 2026-10-13)

Plan 4C is complete (`2026-10-13-phase-4c-self-roles.md`; five tasks and a closing fix wave; three reviews of the prototype and a whole-branch review). It finishes the building of Phase 4. Nothing of it has touched Discord: its staging checklist is at the end of the plan.

**Decided while it was built:** roles fall into groups, pick-one (teams, with "None") or not (regions) (Matt, 2026-10-12); `/iam` alone shows menus, one per group (Matt); the bot writes one role per request and never a member's role list (the security review, agreed by Matt on 2026-10-13: a list built from what an interaction showed gave back a role a moderator had just taken); a role is judged by what it adds to `@everyone`; staff can offer only a role below their own highest, with Manage Roles on the server, or as the owner. From the whole-branch review: a role named `admin`, `moderator` or `bot developer` (the names that make its holder staff of this bot) can never be self-assigned; a role is also judged by what channel overwrites allow it (the controller's ruling, confirmed by Matt on 2026-10-13, as is the rule about staff names: those roles are not self-assignable on his server anyway); at most three changes of one member wait at a time, and one whose interaction has expired is dropped; a member who has not passed the server's screening is refused.

**What only staging can show** (beside the plan's checklist):

- Giving and taking one role for a member who holds roles above the bot or a managed role (a booster): Discord checks only the role in the request, according to its documentation and the discord.js source; nobody has tried it.
- What Discord answers when a role the member already has is given again (expected: success).
- Whether the client resets a member's ticks when the message is edited back to its own menus after a change that did nothing.
- How the menus and the list of menus above them read on a phone.
- A role with a channel overwrite that allows a moderation permission: refused at `add`, marked in `list`. The pass over the server's channels runs on every look at the roles, autocomplete keystrokes included: watch it on a server with many channels.
- A member who has not accepted the server's rules trying `/iam`.
- Discord's real answers for a managed role, a role above the bot and a member who left (the table of error codes has no unit test).

**Left open:**

- The memory of the bot's own recent role changes per member lives fifteen minutes (the life of an interaction token) and has no cap per member; `BoundedMap` bounds the members (2,000) and the throttle the rate.
- In a pick-one group a member can end with two roles when the old one leaves the bot's reach while the new one is being given; the answer says so, and a moderator has to sort it.
- A role renamed on Discord to another row's alias shadows that alias (names are matched before aliases).
- A role name over 50 characters cannot be listed (`Role.roleName` is varchar 50 and a legacy column).
- `/admin self-roles` admits whoever `/admin` admits (admins, moderators, bot developers, the owner); the rule about a staff member's own highest role is what keeps a bot developer from offering a role above them.
- Components V2 would allow a label above each menu instead of the list in the message text; not used.
- A production row whose role is named `admin`, `moderator` or `bot developer` stops being given at cutover (the staff list marks it).
- When a deleted role is recreated and already has a row of its own under another name, the old row of the same name is not taken over: staff remove it first.
- For up to a minute after a database failure `/admin self-roles add` may compare with a stale name (the writes that bring names up to date pause for a minute per server).

**While both bots run on one database, and for a rollback:** the legacy bot names its columns in every statement and never writes `Role.roleSnowflake` or `Role.groupId`, so `!iam`, `!asar` and `!rsar` keep working; a row it inserts is linked by the new bot the first time it is resolved, and a rename written by the new bot is what lets the legacy bot find a renamed role again. The legacy `!asar` goes around the new bot's rule about a staff member's own highest role, and an alias dropped through the new bot takes a legacy `AutoAssignRole` row that points at it along (as in the legacy bot). A rollback needs nothing: the two columns and `RoleGroup` are ignored by the legacy bot. `AutoAssignRole` is neither read nor written.

**Phase 5, decided with Matt on 2026-10-13 before its first brief:** three plans, 5A gyms, 5B regions (KML), 5C boss overrides, member settings and the role-authorization export. `/gym` is hidden from members the way `/admin` is (`default_member_permissions`: Discord shows it only to whoever has Manage Server, or to whom a server admin grants it under Integrations, where it can also be limited to a channel; a bot cannot hide a command per channel by itself), so its autocomplete is not reachable by ordinary members either; staff who use `/gym` in a raid's post to change that raid's gym are pointed to `/location`. For the Phase 8 migration guide (Matt, 2026-10-13): after the move, `/admin`, `/gym` and the other staff commands are shown only to members with Manage Server; a moderator who is staff by role name alone needs the command granted to their role under Server Settings, Integrations, Professor Pine (the bot's own check by role name still applies on top). The gym export stays, as one TSV file of the server's gyms (name, longitude, latitude) for laying over a map, without the split by EX status; it is not important, and it goes once Pine has a public web page that shows the same (an idea only: a public page makes deployment harder). Deleting a gym is refused while an active raid is there and otherwise takes it off train routes, saved routes and gym targets; `import-gyms` and the queue display are dropped; regions get an explicit removal and are never deleted at start; a unique key on a region's channel.

**Phase 5 (admin):** the commands that add or delete a region should remind staff of that region's role (`/admin self-roles add` and its group); sorting the roles into groups is a one-time step at the move to the new bot.

**Phase 8:** the admin migration guide gets: switch `features.roles` on, give the bot Manage Roles and put its role above the roles it hands out, run `/admin self-roles list` on each server before members use `/iam` (every legacy row is linked, at its first use, to whatever role has its name that day; the list marks every row that cannot be given and why), sort the roles into groups (`/admin self-roles group`, then `add` with `group`). `RoleGroup` joins the list of tables to rename when production's lower-case table names are dealt with.

## From Phase 5A, while it was being prototyped (Matt, 2026-10-13)

- **EX becomes elite for gyms** (in plan 5A): the game now calls a former EX gym an "elite raid gym". Every reader-facing "EX" about a gym says so, a gym counts when either stored flag is set, `/gym elite gym eligible`, and the channel setting is `channels['elite-gym-raids']` (default name `elite-gym-raids`). The database columns and the identifiers stored in a party keep their names. **Phase 8, migration guide:** rename the server's `ex-gym-raids` channel to `elite-gym-raids` (or set the name in the settings).
- **The EX raid tier is to be removed altogether** (Matt: there has been no EX raid since early 2020, and Niantic in effect turned EX into elite). Not in 5A (it touches the tier list, the wants codec, raid durations, the feed's headings, the settings and the renderers): its own small plan after 5A. To settle when it is written: a stored EX-tier want becomes an elite-tier want by a migration that rewrites the row's tier code and removes the row when the member already has the elite want (Matt, 2026-10-13: explicit, not read-as; production has no such row, the legacy bot had no code for EX wants, so only databases the new bot has written to are touched), what an `exclusive` heading of the feed does (reported as unknown, like any heading Pine does not know), the settings keys for EX durations (dropped from the schema, tolerated in an old file), and the rollback note for the legacy bot's tier codes.
- **Google key:** production's key works for Geocoding, Static Maps and, since 2026-10-13, Places API (New). **Phase 8, migration guide:** "Places API (New)" must be enabled on the project and also ticked in the key's API restrictions (the second step is easy to miss; the refusal then reads "requests to this API are blocked").

## From the Phase 4 staging run (2026-10-13; one account so far; `var/stage/results.md`, bot-6.log)

Everything one account can do in 4A and 4B passed, with no warning or error in the log. Decided by Matt the same day, to be done in one small fix plan after 5A (together with the removal of the EX tier):

- **`/want` autocomplete order.** Current raid bosses always first; then legendary, mythical and ultra-beast species before the rest (the game master's class, if it says so); a match by a word's beginning before anything looser. Seen: `gira` lists Girafarig before Giratina; `star` lists the three tiers T1, T3, T5 before the boss Starmie and ends with Pikachu and Tyranitar (not explained yet: find out why they match at all); `shadow` ends with Scatterbug, Spewpa and Vivillon (Meadow Pattern), probably "meadow" read as a misspelling of "shadow"; `perfect` adds Zygarde forms, `hundo` adds Iron Hands. Matt (2026-10-13): the tiers match `star` because a tier is also called "3-star"; that is fine, but current bosses come before the tiers. The misspelling guesses ("meadow" for `shadow`) may stay as long as every proper match stands before them and their labels show why they matched (check that Scatterbug and Spewpa say "(Meadow Pattern)" there).
- **The label's own words must find the choice**: `tyranitar base` finds nothing though the choice reads "Tyranitar (base form)" (likewise "every form").
- **`/wild` offers what is never wild**: Black Kyurem and White Kyurem (fusions); megas are already left out. Matt (2026-10-13): no legendary (the one exception: the lake trio, Uxie, Mesprit and Azelf, which do spawn; the Galarian birds stay refused; regional Pokémon, which spawn only in parts of the world, are not restricted: the game master does not say where, and a Pine instance may be anywhere), no mythical, no ultra beast. The game master says so per species (`pokemonSettings.pokemonClass`: `POKEMON_CLASS_LEGENDARY` 77 species, `POKEMON_CLASS_MYTHIC` 23, `POKEMON_CLASS_ULTRA_BEAST` 11 in the copy of `var/game-data-source/`); the parser does not keep it yet (a new field of `PokemonEntry`, so a bump of `GAME_DATA_VERSION`). The same field gives the autocomplete order of `/want` (legendaries first). The exceptions are a short list in the code; a refused choice is refused with a sentence, not only left out of the list.
- **`details` is required for `/wild`** (Matt, 2026-10-13, after the DM tests: a report without it says nothing of where the Pokémon is). Described as where it is; the option order becomes `pokemon`, `details`, then `iv`; the help and its examples follow.
- **`/wild`'s private answer names the channel twice** (the channel mention, then the message link, which Discord also shows as the channel's name): say it once.
- **Privacy and auto-status apply to trains too** (Matt): a train's maker with `auto-status None` is not signed up, with another status signed up as that; with `privacy Anonymous` the post names no maker ("Created by"). Meetups stay as they are. The sentences of `/settings auto-status` and `privacy` then speak of raids and trains. (A `/wild` report keeps naming its reporter.)
- **Speed**: about 0.6 to 0.7 s per command that touches the database (settings 686 ms average, want 602, target 742, wild 699, raid 1,420; `/help` 229), on the laptop with MySQL in Docker. Investigate where the time goes (number of round trips per command, the `User`/`Guild` row made before each transaction, a connection made per query) before blaming the machine.
- **Unown's picture** (a black icon on the dark embed) is a known issue of the transparent icons: lived with.

Smaller findings of that run, to be fixed in the same plan except the last two (Matt, 2026-10-13): the shadow want's sentence ("a Shadow Mewtwo raid … Every form of Mewtwo counts"); `/nickname find` echoes the name as typed, not as registered; `/untarget`'s list does not name a gym's region though `/target`'s does; `/help wild` does not mention the five reports per half hour; no log line says the icon list was fetched; `/unwant` shows the scope only where it could differ (reads well; the checklist was wrong). "EX raids" as a choice of `/want` goes with the EX tier.

Not run yet: everything with the second account (all DMs, `/shout` pings, `/friend-code show` of a member with a code), `features.notifications` off (a restart), the bot's DM, a phone, the Unown channel, Embed Links taken from the bot, a graceful stop with a spawn report standing.

**4C, one account (same day, `var/stage/results.md`):** everything tried passed (staff add, groups, list, the four refusals, a renamed and a deleted role, the bot without Manage Roles; `/iam` by name, alias and menus, pick-one, `/iamnot`; a role given and taken for a member who holds a role above the bot). Decided by Matt, for the same fix plan:

- **A menu gives only what was shown unticked and is now ticked** (as it already takes only what was shown ticked and is now unticked): an old `/iam` message gave back a role the member had given up elsewhere meanwhile.
- **Left as it is:** without Manage Roles a pick in an open `/iam` message replaces the message by the sentence and the menus are gone (the member runs `/iam` again once staff fixed the bot's role).
- **Wording:** `list` counts only the roles that can be given in its heading (or says how many cannot); `remove` of a role that no longer exists does not say "whoever holds it keeps it".
- Not run: more than 25 roles in a group and more than five menus (unit tests cover the splitting), the two moderator limits, two-factor, a phone, `features.roles` off, the legacy bot on the same database, two quick presses (the client locks a menu while its interaction is pending).
- Seen beside it: after a reload of the web client older lines read "(@unknown-user)" for a member and "# unknown" for a post (known; a client matter).

## From Phase 5A (gym administration, 2026-10-13)

Built as `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-10-13-phase-5a-gyms.md` says (its fourteen decisions are at its top); the closing fix wave added that the 10 metre look happens under the lock and that no server's staff administer another server's gyms (Matt, 2026-10-13). Nothing has touched Discord; the staging checklist is at the end of the plan.

**Staging must show** (beyond the checklist): the link Pokémon GO shares read at the first hop (tried by hand on 2026-10-13: both of Matt's links redirect to `maps.google.com/?q=<lat>,<lon>` and match production's gyms to the last digit); Google really asked after an add (the key in `.env` has the Geocoding API, Maps Static API and Places API (New) enabled and allowed); the hourly mend of a train whose stop's gym is gone (delete a gym's row by hand on the test database, wait for the upkeep or restart, see the line in the train's post); `/gym` not offered to an ordinary member; with a second server, that a gym of the other server is neither offered nor accepted.

**Left open:**

- `/gym`'s `gym` option asks the database for the server's row id (`ctx.guilds.idFor`) on every use and every keystroke of its autocomplete. A server's row id never changes: keep it in memory (belongs to the look at speed in "From the Phase 4 staging run").
- The option's search runs without a limit and cuts afterwards, so that gyms out of reach do not use up the 25 choices; fine at 3,200 gyms (a search is a few milliseconds).
- Names of another server's gyms can still show in two places: the "within 10 metres" lines of a question right at a border, and "nearest gym" in the gym view.
- If the directory's re-read fails inside `add`, the new gym is not in memory until the next load, and a second add at that place would not see it (the failure is logged).
- A gym target added in the instant between the deletion's transaction and the directory forgetting the gym leaves an orphan `GymNotification` row, which every reader skips.
- The announcement of a new or deleted gym goes only where the bot may post embeds; a text fallback (the deletion is one line and needs none) is not built: it needs a gateway method the fakes do not have.
- `plain()` leaves some asterisks of a long run unescaped (`*********`): bold or italics may toggle in staff-typed text; cosmetic, older than this plan (discord.js `escapeMarkdown`).
- `game-data-service.writes.test.ts` ("never overlap…") failed once in a full run beside other work and passed alone: load-sensitive, to look at if it shows again.
- The 411 production gyms that lie in no region: any server's staff may touch them (nothing says whose they are); worth a look when regions are next edited (5B).
- The intermediate commits of the plan's Tasks 5 and 6 already carry the request variants and texts of the later subcommands.

**5B (regions):** `/region import|bounds|remove` as decided (follow-ups, "Phase 5, decided with Matt"); after regions change, `GymDirectory.load()` and `RegionDirectory` both, and the cached region picture goes by itself (`ImageCache` names by content); remind staff of the region's self-role; a region that is removed leaves its gyms in no region (see above).

**5C:** `/help command:<group name>`; the owner in DMs for the staff guards (moot while every staff command is servers only); `/admin boss`, `/admin member-settings`, the role-authorization export.

**Phase 8 (cutover, the admin migration guide):**

- Rename the server's `ex-gym-raids` channel (production: `potential-ex-gym-raids`) to `elite-gym-raids`, or set `channels['elite-gym-raids']` to its name: a channel that is not found gets no copies, silently. An old settings file with the old key is refused at start (the schema is strict).
- The Google key needs the Geocoding API, Maps Static API and **Places API (New)** enabled on the project and ticked in the key's API restrictions.
- Keep the application non-public ("Public Bot" off in the Developer Portal): staff of any server the bot is in may add gyms and touch gyms that lie in no region.
- Tell staff how to give a location: in Pokémon GO open the gym, "View in map", share the link, paste it into `/gym add` or `/gym move`.
- `/gym` and `/admin` are shown only to members with Manage Server unless granted under Integrations (already noted in "From Phase 4C").

## From Phase 4F (the staging fixes and the end of the EX tier, 2026-10-13)

Built as `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-10-13-phase-4f-staging-fixes.md` says (its eleven decisions are at its top); the closing fix wave added that a name typed in full brings no guesses, that after `shadow` only species found by name are offered, and that `/wild`'s list has no class preference. Nothing has touched Discord; the staging checklist is at the end of the plan (`deploy-commands` first: `/wild` has a required option).

**Speed (not solved here):** a round trip to the database takes under a millisecond and the pool reuses its connections; `/settings` makes one statement and took 686 ms in staging, `/wild` none before its answer and took 699. The time is not in the database. Inference, to be measured at the next staging: every slow command defers, so it makes two requests to Discord (the deferral, the edit of the answer) where `/help` makes one. If the log's times confirm it, short commands could answer without deferring (Matt decides; the catch is the three seconds an undeferred command has).

**Left open:**

- Bare `s`, `sh`, `sha`, `shad` in `/want` list every shadow boss and the three shadow tiers before any Pokémon; with twenty shadow bosses `sha` leaves two places. Look at it in staging with a real feed (a cap, or behind name-led hits unless the text is `shadow`).
- A nickname of a legendary lifts it for every beginning of that nickname: `sa` gives Ho-Oh first (`santa`), `a` Giratina (`altered`). The curated nicknames `mega` and `altered` only repeat a name's word and could go; megas of a classed species stand before their species (`me`: Mega Mewtwo X and Y before Mew in a train's `/boss`).
- `mega` hides Meganium and `alo` Alomomola until one more letter (a whole word before a beginning).
- Guesses are ordered by score, not by closeness (`latois` gives Latias before Latios), and a six-letter word may be read as a four-letter one. `raid` offers "Urshifu (Rapid Strike Style)" in `/want` and a train's `/boss` (a guess the label shows).
- A short word that lost its first letter (`engar`) finds nothing (ruled).
- One typed letter lists that Unown letter first in `/want` and a train's `/boss`.
- The first start after the upgrade needs the network (the game data cache is version 4); offline it stops saying there is no saved copy although an older one is on disk: the message could say so.
- A server whose `Guild` row the legacy bot deletes under the running new bot: reads by the remembered id answer from memory until a write or `ensure` corrects it. Not at cutover (the legacy bot is stopped first).
- `NewTrainRepository`, `SavedRouteRepository` and `SelfRoleRepository` do not share `RowIds`.
- A forum made before this plan keeps its "EX" tag (`ensureTags` only adds).
- A member who pressed Here on a train without a route stays Here when the first stop is added.
- Other options with the trap `/wild` had (optional free text after an autocomplete option): only `/raid time`, left optional on Matt's word, and naturally optional staff options.

**Phase 8 (cutover, the admin migration guide):** delete the forums' "EX" tag by hand if it is in the way; `wildSpawnExceptions` in the settings (add the Galarian birds if the game makes them wild spawns); an old settings file with the EX duration keys starts all the same; the former EX want code (-21, only ever written by the new bot) is no longer known: such a row is skipped and counted.

## From the staging run of 5A and 4F (2026-10-13, one account)

Run with Matt's own account (the owner, so staff) on the test server; the log is `var/stage/logs/bot-7.log`, without a warning or an error. Migration 009 ran and the game data was downloaded anew.

**Passed, 4F:** the lists of `/want` (`gira`, `star`, `ty`, `arc`, `tyranitar base`, one letter); `/wild` (details required, Mewtwo, a mega and Black Kyurem refused each with its sentence, Azelf accepted, the channel named once, the details in the report, `/help wild`); a train made with auto-status None and privacy Anonymous (nobody signed up, no "Created by", the answer says to sign up, the unsigned maker added stops) and with Here (signed up as Coming, "Created by"); the role menus (a role given up in one menu did not come back from a stale second one); `/nickname find` (the name as registered); `/untarget`; no EX among the tiers of `/raid` and `/want`.

**Passed, 5A:** `/gym add` with coordinates (the question, the region, the map, the nearest gym; Google gave the address and nearby places within a minute), the second add at the same place (the gym 1 m away, "Add anyway"), `/raid` finding the new gym at once, `/gym elite` (the post says "elite raid gym", the copy in `elite-gym-raids`), `/gym edit` (the form at once and pre-filled; a notice of two lines shown in the raid's post with its line break), `/gym move` (4 m with a raid there; 1.1 km asks, Cancel keeps; in a raid's post the pointer to `/location`), `/gym delete` (refused with the link while the raid was active; afterwards the question listing the train and the gym target, the stop taken off the train with a line in its post, a double click showing the result), `/gym refresh`, `/gym export` (the file was not opened), `/admin clear-image-cache`.

**Speed, measured** (`lateMs` and `deferMs` of the router's debug line; 41 deferred commands): an interaction arrives about 65 ms after it was made; one request to Discord takes 220 to 280 ms from here (the deferral averaged 273). A command that does not defer answers in 100 to 240 ms (`/help`, `/route add`, `/gym edit`'s form); a short deferred one in 450 to 700 (`/settings`, `/want`, `/target`, `/nickname`), that is its two requests; `/raid` and `/train` take about 1.2 s (the deferral, the post, the link line, the answer). The database is not seen in these numbers, except in the first write of a process (`/want` 912 ms, then 533). So answering short commands without deferring would about halve what a member waits for them. Matt decides; the catch is unchanged (three seconds for an undeferred answer, so only commands whose work is a few database statements).

**Found, to fix:**

- `/want shadow` still offers three guesses ("Meadow Pattern" of Scatterbug, Spewpa and Vivillon, read as a misspelling of `shadow`): a text that is a fixed word in full should bring no guesses.
- Spawn reports are not removed after a restart (the reports of the afternoon were still in the channel): the timer lives in memory. Matt asked for it (2026-10-13): keep each report (channel, message, when it is due) in a table, delete what is overdue at start and with the hourly upkeep.
- `/gym show` prints Google's address parts raw (`street_number: 2982 2950 2918 2917`, `political: …`): they are search words, not an address. Show one readable line (or nothing) and keep the parts for the search.
- The lines in a train's post name an anonymous maker who arranges the route ("**Googer** added stop 1 to the route"). Matt (2026-10-13): an anonymous maker is not named there; the line says what happened ("Stop 1 was added to the route: …").

Matt (2026-10-13): these four are fixed in one small round before 5B. Matt (2026-10-13) also agreed to the late deferral, as a fifth item of that round: a short command (`/settings`, the wants and gym targets, `/nickname`, `/friend-code`, `/iam` by name) starts its work at once and answers directly when it is done within about a second; only then does the bot defer and deliver the answer afterwards. `/raid`, `/train` and the gym commands keep deferring at once.

**Links in `/gym add`, tried afterwards:** the long form of what Pokémon GO shares (`maps.google.com/?q=<lat>,<lon>&entry=gps…&shorturl=1`) reads as an exact point; a link with only a map centre (`/maps/@<lat>,<lon>,17z`) reads with the warning; a real short link shared from Google Maps for a named place (`maps.app.goo.gl/…`) was followed (about 0.65 s) to `/maps/place/<name and street address>/data=!4m2!3m1!1s0x…:0x…`, which holds no coordinates, and got the sentence that says to paste coordinates, as intended. Matt (2026-10-13): it stays so; such a link is not geocoded from its address, since that is less exact and not the preferred way to give a gym's place. He is looking into having the Scopely Wayfarer site export the true list of gyms (a research job to come; nothing started), which would make typed and pasted places the exception. A link shared by the game itself from a phone was not tried.

**Not run:** "Add with details…", a keyword of more than 50 characters and "Open the form again"; two forms at once; `/gym show` with every text and typed `#id` or a bare number; `/gym elite` back to False; the announcement of a new or deleted gym (the test server has no `bot-updates` channel); `/admin self-roles list` with a role that cannot be given; the second account with Manage Server but no staff role; how the gym view reads on a phone.

**Passed with the second account (Tester, the same evening):** `/gym` is not offered to a member; the new-train DM of an anonymous maker names nobody; a maker with auto-status None who is not staff adds a stop and is told to sign up when pressing "Move train…"; after the deletion of a gym `/targets` no longer lists it (the question had counted the one member).

**Left on the test server:** an ordinary chat message "Here" of Matt's account in the closed post "Train · Stage 4F anonymous" (a slip of the driver), and an unanswered question "Add Stage Twin" (private, expires by itself). The two test trains, the test raid and the test gym (#11) are closed or deleted; Matt's settings are back to Interested and Visible.

## From the fix round after the staging of 5A and 4F (2026-10-13)

Built by the controller in eight commits (36f8524 to 11f2977), without a plan document or prototype, with one review of the whole diff (no critical finding; its important one, the start-time DM naming an anonymous maker, is fixed). Full check, build and database suite ran on the tip. Nothing has touched Discord.
