## From the /rank fixes (Matt's findings on the dev bot, 2026-10-23)

- **Done:**
  - The evolution line starts at the first stage, and each species is followed by its megas. Malamar's is Inkay, Malamar, Mega Malamar; it used to say "does not evolve".
  - The ranks are pvpivs.com's: equal in all 40 million rows of the site's list, every league, both caps. That includes a flaw of the site, copied on purpose (`siteKey` in `src/domain/pvp/rank.ts`): in a tie of the stat product, an attack below 100 comes above one of 100 or more.
  - Super Megas (mega level 4): a mega the game master gives `selfCpBoostAdditionalLevel` (2 for 11 species in October 2026; Primals none) is also ranked up to 50 and 51 plus those levels. Each extra cap is shown with its own pvpivs.com link where it changes the standing. That is in Master League for every Super Mega, and in Ultra and Great League for none of them yet. New ones come with the game master's refresh, at events.
  - The pvpivs.com links use the site's own names, which it needs exactly; forms, megas and two-word names opened an empty page before. The link goes up to level 50, with two links (50 and 51) when level 51 is shown.
  - The answer redesigned with Matt (design `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-23-rank-answer-redesign-design.md`, mockups beside it): the family's members beside their pictures while the message holds them (else a list), each level heading ending in a 🔗 link to pvpivs.com, icon-only league buttons, a "🧬 Family" toggle, and a menu "Show another of the family…".
  - Families fold the forms that only change the look into their species: Burmy's family is Burmy, the three Wormadam, Mothim.
  - A mega that PokeMiners' icon list lacks shows its portrait from the Mega Portraits folder (13 of 61, the newest).
- **Waiting on pvpivs.com:** Matt posted the bug report (`var/pvpivs-bug-report.md`, git-ignored) in the site's Discord bug forum on 2026-10-23. When the site fixes it, compare the attack stat itself after the product in `betterFirst`; the Bulbasaur test in `rank.test.ts` then flips.
- **For staging:**
  - Upload the four league emoji first (`npm run emoji:upload -- --apply`, Matt), then deploy the commands (the `evolutions` option's description changed).
  - Check that `[🔗](<…>)` in a level heading shows as a link on desktop and on a phone. If Discord does not take an emoji as a link's text, use `[link](<…>)`.
  - Check the league icons on the buttons and in the heading; a member who is not the asker picking in the menu (Discord may keep showing their pick in the public menu until it is redrawn); Eevee's family as a list; Mega Malamar's portrait; Burmy's family of five.
  - Then check a link of a form (Giratina Origin), of a mega (Mega Malamar) and one with two caps (Medicham 15/15/15 in Great League). For a Super Mega, check Mega Malamar 10/12/13 in Master League: four caps (50, 51, 52, 53), each with its link.
- **To watch (nobody has measured it yet):** whether a Super Mega's two levels stack with a best buddy's one. Pine assumes they do (53): Matt, 2026-10-23, says a best buddy's boost has always stacked on every such bonus before. PvPoke models 52 and no best buddy. If the game says otherwise, drop `superMegaBuddy` in `cappedRanks`.
- **Staged on the dev bot (2026-10-23, logs `bot-37`, `bot-38`):** all pass in `#pvp-chat-and-tournaments`:
  - Malamar's family in Master League with a picture per member (Mega Malamar's portrait), ▸ on Malamar, the league icons on the buttons and in the heading, the 🔗 in each heading clickable and leading to the right pvpivs.com page.
  - Picking Inkay in the menu redrew the answer for Inkay, league and family kept. Eevee's family in Great League as a list, ranks as the mockups had them. Burmy (Sandy Cloak)'s family of five, ▸ on Burmy.

The second-account step passes (Matt, Android and web alike): another member picking Inkay got a private copy, the public answer stayed on Malamar. Their own client then shows their pick in the public menu until the message is redrawn, and a restart of the app drops the private copy: Discord's behaviour for every bot, accepted as designed.
- **Idea, not planned (Matt, 2026-10-23):** an answer that shows the ranks in all leagues at once. Not worth it while the league buttons switch in one press.

  - The share of the best is cut to two places, where pvpivs.com rounds it, so only the best shows 100%. It can read 0.01 lower than the site.
  - PvPoke ranks by the exact product with no tie-break and has default IV floors of its own, so its ranks can differ from Pine's (and from pvpivs.com's) in ties.
  - 37 of the 1,431 entries of the fixture have no name on pvpivs.com: the site does not list them or marks them `_SPECULATIVE`.


## From the /rank IV floors (2026-10-29)

- **Done** (design `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-29-rank-iv-floors-design.md`, agreed with Matt and reviewed): without a `filter`, `/rank` works the lowest IVs out from the game master. A shadow legendary or mythical (the new `shadow:` option) starts at 6; a legendary or mythical that cannot be traded, of a species that cannot either, at 10 (Mew, Zarude, Deoxys, Diancie, Eternatus, Zygarde); nothing else, legendaries included (wild ones have come with none). No lowest levels (Matt: events have broken every one). IVs below the floor are ranked among all with a sentence. The pvpivs.com links always carry the floor and the levels.
- **For staging:** deploy the commands first (`shadow:` is new). Check Mew without a filter (of 216 and its small print), Mew 3/15/15 (of 4,096 and the fallback's sentence), `filter: None` for Mew, a shadow Mewtwo (of 1,000, "Shadow" in the head and the family), `shadow: True` for a mega refused, a link opening pvpivs.com with the same floor even after picking another floor on the site.
- **Not done, by Matt's choice:** pvpivs.com's floor of 1 for tradable legendaries and its lowest levels (15, 20, 8).
- **Staged** (2026-10-29 evening, bot-40.log, Googer driven by Claude in #pvp-chat-and-tournaments, commands deployed by Matt). Every step run passes:
  - `/great` Mew 10/12/13 without a filter: "Rank 35 of 216 · 98.66%" and "IVs from 10: Mew cannot be traded…". Its link (`f=10&min=1&max=50`) opens pvpivs.com at 35 of 216.
  - Mew 3/15/15: "of 4,096", "Ranked among all IVs: 3/15/15 is below the 10 Pine assumes for Mew.", `f=0`.
  - `shadow: True` Mewtwo 10/12/13: "Shadow Mewtwo 10/12/13", "Rank 712 of 1,000 · 94.27%", "IVs from 6: a shadow legendary comes from a raid or Giovanni.". Its link (`IVs=10_12_13_true&f=6`) gives on pvpivs.com the same row: 712, level 12.5, 1451 CP, 94.27%.
  - `shadow: True` Mega Mewtwo X: refused privately, "Mega Mewtwo X has no shadow form in the game."
  - Not run: `filter: None` for Mew (covered by the unit tests).
- **Seen in passing:** pvpivs.com shows a shadow's attack and defense with the shadow's ×1.2 and ×0.83 in brackets (146.4 (175.7)); Pine shows only the plain stats. The rank is the same. Kept as it is: the shadow bonus does not change the attack stat itself, and the true value is the one used for CMP (Matt, 2026-10-29).
## From Phase 8R-6 (pictures at the stops, closed 2026-10-24)

- **Staging:** `2026-10-24-phase-8r6-staging.md` ran on 2026-10-24 (see "From the staging run of 8R-6"). No migration and no deploy.
- **Matt’s decisions after the design review:**
  - the post is renamed "Today’s Stops" (curly apostrophe, as all of the bot’s text);
  - under an event’s window the day’s research stays in the post as "Back at <time>, after <event>", without pictures, after the hideouts;
  - on a busy day pictures are dropped step by step (items, then one per task, then none) before any reported task is cut;
  - "Already reported" is given back up to the window’s uses (10 in 30 minutes), then counts again, and the help says so.
- **Defaults taken in the build, for Matt to object to:**
  - `StopPictures` waits at most 2.5 s for missing pictures; one that lands later is used at the next redraw, and nothing is redrawn when it lands.
  - At most 2,000 pictures are kept in `var/pictures/` (512 KB and 1024 px a side at most; png, jpeg, webp, gif), renewed after 7 days in the background.
  - A failed fetch is not tried again for 30 minutes, and a 404 or 410 shows no picture until a later fetch works.
  - The encounter picture is pinned to PokeMiners’ commit c2a686eb.
  - The map’s hideouts show the post’s square picture.
  - The summary names waiting tasks apart: "· 13 tasks back at <time>".
- **Left for later:**
  - The map’s catches and rewards still use the feeds’ addresses (only hideouts use the squares): for the map’s UX pass.
  - The map’s `waiting` count is unchanged.
  - Whether `app.ts` calls `picturesServed` and `stopReportSteps` is seen only by reading it, since `startApp` is not unit-tested.

## From the staging run of 8R-6 (2026-10-24 morning, Googer driven by Claude, Tester by Matt; bot-8r6-1.log and bot-8r6-2.log)

The main checkout at typescript-migration 9f08a94b, no deploy. Settings `var/stage/settings-8r6.json`: the stage settings with `web.publicUrl` a Cloudflare quick tunnel (`cloudflared tunnel --url http://127.0.0.1:8080`, no account; Matt downloaded `cloudflared.exe` to `Downloads\cloudflare-tunnel`) and `trustProxy`; for the second start `raidEvents.url` pointed at the made-up feed. The map was logged in to through `/maps`' link (Discord's OAuth redirect is registered for localhost only). Region #oakland. Tasks used: "Make 3 Excellent Throws in a row" (Larvitar, Beldum, Gible), "Catch 25 Pokémon" (Rare Candy), "Catch 7 Pokémon"; Cliff, Giovanni (Zekrom), the decoy (Bellsprout), the water grunts' two lineups. New staging tools in `var/stage/`: `msg-pics.mjs` (a message's text and pictures), `dm-pics.mjs` (the bot's DMs to a member), `serve-events.mjs` (ScrapedDuck's feed on 127.0.0.1:8098 with invented events, `--drop <eventID>`) and `fake-events.mjs` (`window`, `big`, `rotation`, `clear`). Running notes: `var/stage/8r6-staging-notes.md`.

**Passes:** 2–14, 16–19, 21 (live, see F1).
- The post: a picture per reward line, squares whole (Larvitar's head and feet), the encounter picture for "one of", Rare Candy, Cliff's and Giovanni's portraits, the decoy's Bellsprout, all from `<publicUrl>/pictures/<key>.png?v=…`; the summary links "Today’s Stops". Matt: the posts look good on mobile too.
- DMs: the encounter picture for "one of" and "not sure which", Gible in the narrowing's confirmation, "Rare Candy · Research", portraits for Cliff and Giovanni.
- The grunt message: one thumbnail by its heading, the stop's photo by "Where", no gallery; narrowed in place to Magikarp/Feebas (it keeps the encounter picture: two catches), the DM unchanged, the answer "Changed in #oakland".
- "Already reported" three times on Discord and three times on the map's POST (409); then six new reports went through and the seventh was refused (429): exactly ten counted, all six given back.
- Serving: after a restart the same picture addresses and no fetch in the log. `/api/stop-reports` gives the hideouts' squares.
- Mid-day: the invented "Staging Research Hour" (10:29–10:59): at its start the event's line, the hideouts, then "Back at 10:59, after Staging Research Hour" with the day's tasks as text; Tester's report inside it "until 10:59 AM", with its picture; the summary "1 task · 3 hideouts · 2 tasks back at 10:59"; at its end the normal day again with pictures.

**Not run:** 15 (optional, without a public address); 20 (a big event's daily hours: Matt, redundant with 18–19); 12 in #squirrelbreeze (the bot may pin there again).

**F1 (Important): an in-person City Safari ends every server's research period.** ScrapedDuck lists "Pokémon GO City Safari: Boston (Rescheduled)" (`pokemon-go-city-safari-boston-2026-rescheduled`, eventType `city-safari`, local times without a zone, `hasFieldResearchTasks`), and Pine read it as a research event for everyone: 2026-10-24 10:00 to 10-25 18:00. At 10:00 Pittsburgh's research went for good, under "City Safari: Boston (Rescheduled): its tasks until midnight". `parseResearchEventFeed` leaves out only in-person editions with zoned (UTC) times. Fix: leave out in-person event types (`city-safari`, and the like: check the feed's types) whatever their times. Until it is fixed, a dev bot started on ScrapedDuck's feed before 10-25 18:00 cuts the research again; the row was deleted on the dev database, and the made-up feed dropped it.

**Matt's enhancement E1: a map link per stop in "Today’s Stops".** Keep the reporter's name (trust, and the moderators acting on a false report) unless the line turns out too cluttered. A Google Maps link costs about 80 characters a stop line against the post's text budget; a short link through the bot's web server (`<publicUrl>/m/<stop>`) about 45.

**Matt's game facts G1: which research can be reported during events.**
- Week-long multi-day events: about half normal research, half the event's.
- Short events (Community Day and the like): almost only the event's (95%+).
- Long-weekend events (GO Fest and the like): only the event's. The three major ones (Tour in February, Fest in summer, Wild Area in November) reset the tasks on the clock hour through the core hours (typically 10 AM to 6 PM), and the task set differs by hour: four sets in turn (10–11, 11–12, 12–1, 1–2), then again from 2–3, so each set runs twice.
- Matt: offering the event's tasks first covers every case; the major events also need the hourly reset. Today the event's own tasks are not known (design 4.1: only the normal research page is read; a missing task is typed in full) and a big event's hours are one window a day. The hourly reset can come from the feed's hours alone; the hourly task sets need the event's Leek Duck page parsed. First look at this year's GO Fest page (Matt: Wild Area 2026's page will not have the details yet).


**G1 research (2026-10-24, through Leek Duck's pages):** ScrapedDuck keeps no event tasks (only `hasSpawns` and `hasFieldResearchTasks`) and no core hours. Leek Duck has a research page per big event (`leekduck.com/gofest-2026/research/`, a new address each year): day tabs, "10:00 a.m. – 7:00 p.m. local time", a heading per type hour, two tasks each. GO Fest 2026 Global was **not** four sets in turn: nine different type hours a day (Saturday Ice, Electric, Fire, Psychic, Ghost, Water, Flying, Rock, Dragon; Sunday Ground, Steel, Normal, Poison, Bug, Grass, Dark, Fairy, Fighting). The four-set cycle may be Tour's or older events': unverified, so no cycle is hard-coded. Without reading the page: reset the reports every clock hour inside a big event's hours. G1 is now only these hourly sets of the major events.

Step (2) passes on Matt's phone: Googer reported "Catch 25 Pokémon" (Rare Candy) at Cathedral of Learning Lawn and a water grunt at Historic Landmark Cathedral of Learning Plaque in #oakland; the stop names link to Google Maps in "Today’s Stops" and in Tester's research and Rocket DMs (both `direct messages sent`), and the grunt message keeps its Where with `Map`. A plan of its own: read an event page's "Raid Hour" section as the spawns are. Answered (Leek Duck, 2026-10-25): yes, the Wednesday Raid Hour is gone that week. Leek Duck's events list has Raid Hours on 10-28, 11-04, 11-18 and 11-25, none on 11-11, and Wednesday 11-11 6–7 PM is instead the event's five-star Max Battle hour of Registeel (Regidrago Thursday, Latias and Latios Friday). So the event page's Raid Hours replace the week's Wednesday one, and its Max Battle hours need reading too. **Decided (Matt, 2026-10-25):** Pine reads an event page's Raid Hours and Max Battle hours, and in a week whose event has its own Raid Hours assumes no Wednesday one. ScrapedDuck PR 33 (`extraData.raidSchedule` for `event` items, open and unmerged since 2025-11) would carry this too, but is a year old against Leek Duck's pages.
- **Question kept:** the research confirmation sentence ("one of" narrowed) names its stops plain, without links (decision 5 of the prototype). **Answered (Matt, 2026-10-25):** they get the map link as the other DMs; done in plan 8R-8 (branch `event-tasks-first`), with an event's research tasks first in the pick lists and in "Today’s Stops".

## From the live run of 2026-10-24 (Matt on the D-max Lake Trio day: the dev bot as members would use it; notes `var/stage/live-2026-10-24-notes.md`, bot-live-1.log)

- **L1 (before cutover, Matt): `/counters` for a max battle shows Pokebattler's suggested team, but links to and names Pokebattler's role rankings.** For D-max Azelf the "Charger" was Blissey: Pine asks `/max/parties/suggested/defenders/…?attack=2&meterCharge=1`, whose METER_CHARGE slot is Blissey, Registeel, Lugia, Umbreon, Omastar. Pokebattler's own Chargers page (`/max/AZELF/METER_CHARGE`, "fast meter, survives spreads") is a ranking of single Pokémon (`/fights/rankingJobs?request=/raids/defenders/AZELF/levels/RAID_LEVEL_5_MAX/attackers/levels/40/strategies/CINEMATIC_ATTACK_WHEN_POSSIBLE/DEFENSE_RANDOM_MC?sort=<role>&…&numParty=1`): Gengar (Gigantamax), Hydreigon, Gengar, Blastoise; its Defenders page (`/CHARGER`): Hydreigon, Blastoise (Gigantamax), Snorlax (Gigantamax); Blissey tops only its Healers. Fix (recommended): read the ranking job per role, so the list matches the page Pine links to; else keep the team and call it "Pokebattler's suggested team" with a link to its Lobby page. The ranking job API (asynchronous, `redirect=false`) needs a look first.
- **L1 fixed by 8X-I (2026-10-24, branch `counters-roles`).** Pokebattler's own Lobby page (`/max/<boss>/PARTY`) uses the same suggested-team builder Pine asks and calls Blissey a "Charger · Fills the Max Meter fast": Pine's list was right, its "Full results" button linked the single-Pokémon role rankings. Now: the button "Lobby on Pokebattler" opens the Lobby; the answer says "Pokebattler’s suggested team, as its Lobby builds it"; each slot's role has Pokebattler's words in small print (max phase only, fills the Max Meter, charges the meter and takes hits, heals the team, shields and taunts). Matt's decision: Pine's roles are Pokebattler's four trainer roles: Attacker (an attacker and two chargers, the default), Defensive (an attacker and two defenders), Healer (a healer and two defenders), Shielder (three shielders); the old Attackers + charger, Balanced and All attackers read as Attacker until the commands are deployed again. **Deploy the commands** (`/counters`' role choices changed); staging: the 8X-6 checklist's steps 2–3 (rewritten). Open: whether the Lobby can be opened on a given role by its address; the old Healer and Shielder keep their names with other teams.
- **8X-I staged (2026-10-24, 23:36–23:38, Googer on the web, `bot-live-3.log`, commands deployed with `settings-live.json`):** the 8X-6 checklist's steps 2–3 pass. `/counters role:` describes "your role as Pokebattler defines it, Attacker when left out"; the dialog's role menu offers Attacker, Defensive, Healer, Shielder with their teams. D-max Azelf: Attacker Hydreigon + Chargers Blissey, Metagross; Defensive Hydreigon + Defenders Zamazenta (Crowned Shield), Gigalith; Healer Blissey + Defenders Hydreigon, Zamazenta; Shielder Blissey, Hydreigon, Lugia. Each slot has its small-print words, each answer the "Pokebattler’s suggested team, as its Lobby builds it" line, the "Other …" lines, and "Lobby on Pokebattler" opening `https://www.pokebattler.com/max/AZELF/PARTY`. Four `Pokebattler answered`, no warning of an unknown shape. Not run: the old choices on a client without the deploy; steps 4 onwards.

## From Phase 8R-8 (an event's research tasks first, closed 2026-10-25)

Built as a small fix on `event-tasks-first` (brief `.superpowers/sdd/event-tasks-first/brief.md`; Matt's "go", 2026-10-25), test first per task: 54a0f341 the task list, 25a0b75f "Today’s Stops", 86b1b6d2 the confirmation DM, ddce493d docs; whole-branch review on Opus, high: ready, three Minor (8R8-1 a stale example in `notifications-and-dms.md`, 8R8-2 a stale comment, 8R8-3 the headings read as an event's now logged at each read), fixed in 61977f51. Check (9,621 tests) and build green; no repository change, no migration, no command change (no deploy).

- **What it is:** Leek Duck's research page puts an event's tasks first under the event's own heading(s), which do not match the events feed's names (2026-06-10: "Candela's Quest for Victory" and "Spark's Caretaking Quest" for one event; 2026-08-20 "Ultra Unlock: Water Festival"; 2026-09-25 "Choose Your Path: Twilight Trails"). Any heading but the standing ones (`isEventCategory`) is an event's; `eventTasksFirst` puts its tasks first in the list in use (`/research`, the map's pick list, `/want`), and "Today’s Stops" lists them first (each part by most stops), so a busy day cuts ordinary tasks first. The confirmation DM's stops are links to their place (`stopMapLink`), within the DM's 2,400-character room.
- **Staging:** at the next event with research (Into the Wild, from 2026-11-09 10:00): the log's `research tasks of an event read` with the event's heading(s); `/research report` without typing offers the event's tasks first; the map's pick list too; an event task with one stop above ordinary tasks with more in "Today’s Stops"; a "one of" narrowed sends "Confirmed: the reward at <linked stops> is …".
- **Watch:** a heading Leek Duck renames or adds (a new standing category) would read as an event's and lead every list: the log line shows it the first time.

Worked on in `../Professor-Pine-web`, branch `web-polish`.

## From the readers' boss rules (2026-10-25)

Matt's rule after the Lake Trio day (the max reader refused Dynamax Azelf while `/max` took it; b6a9f0b7 patched the reader's own rule): every surface decides a domain fact with the domain code the commands use. Branch `max-boss-reader`. **Not staged yet.**

- **Max battles:** `maxScreenshotReport` decides through `typedMaxBoss`, as `/max`: the name read is looked for among the max bosses `/max` takes (listed, or let battle so by the game) and the species the list has in any form (Gigantamax Toxtricity listed as Amped), every Pokémon on an Eternamax screen; off the list only in a tier of the screen's kind that `typedMaxBoss` allows. The context's `species` and `anyPokemon` are one `pokemon(read, among)`. On exact names the old reader already agreed with `/max` for every Pokémon of the fixture's catalogue; what differed was the search: a cut-short "Kingdr" was read as Kingler (the game lets it battle) beside a listed Kingdra.
- **Raids:** `/raid`'s rule for a tier typed before a name is `bossAtTier` (`src/domain/pogo/boss-at-tier.ts`), its refusals worded in `/raid` as before. The raid reader, when no listed boss fits a hatched raid, takes a Pokémon of the game data whose raid CP at the heads' tier is the CP read (or one digit off) and whose name is nearly exactly the name read, through `bossAtTier`: off the list it is reported and posted as `/raid t5 azelf` would (`Azelf (T5)`), with the doubt `boss-unlisted` in "Screenshot feedback" (so the screenshot is copied to review); listed in another level or as the other shadow kind, or the plain Pokémon in a mega raid, it stays a raid without a boss.
- `ocr:check` before the commit: the research samples and every labelled folder (`var/ocr/matt-*`) against their baselines, 0 from right to wrong; no sample of them shows a boss off the list.
- **Staging:** a hatched raid of a boss the feed does not list (or one staff hid with `/admin boss hide`), posted with a mention: the post names the boss with its tier as `/raid t<n> <name>` would, and its "Screenshot feedback" says "The boss is on no list of current raid bosses: …". A max screen whose species is listed but not a max boss in the game master is read as the listed boss.

## From Phase 8X-J (a boss at the wrong tier moved by one add, closed 2026-10-25)

Built as a small plan on `max-boss-tier` (brief `.superpowers/sdd/8x-j-max-boss-tier/brief.md`, design `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-25-max-boss-tier-fix-design.md`; Matt asleep, decisions taken by the design), test first per task: 6bee6827 the move rule (`tierMoves` in `familyBosses`, `oneTierAmong`, Coming through `coming()`), b58570e3 what staff see, 30d9c80f running battles move, da4b5d7a screenshots tell the moderators (`listTierDoubt`, `ListTierWatch`, `ModerationReports.listTier`); whole-branch review on Opus, high: ready after fixes, J1 Important (raid moves bypassed `RaidChangeService`'s rules: now `moveBossTier` through `apply`) and J2–J10 Minor, all fixed in 581adc6e; re-review ready, its R1 (docs) fixed at the close. Check (9,699 tests), build and db (597) green; `ocr:check` 0 from right to wrong on the research samples and every `var/ocr/matt-*` folder (the reader's `unlistedBossOf` split into `pokemonByCpAndName`, same behaviour). No migration, no command option changed (no deploy).

- **Decisions for Matt to confirm (taken by default):**
  - **Every server's running battles move** with an add (the boss list is installation-wide; the design said "the server's").
  - **The note writes the tier as the choice's name** (`tier:Dynamax 2★`), since Discord filters a choice option by its name; the design's I6 said the choice value (`D2`). The boss is named by its catalogue key (`pokemon:sneasel`), which `/admin boss` reads.
  - **`autoset` moves like `add`**, with the same answer lines and running moves.
  - **Costumed bosses never move and are never left out** (costumed and plain forms share a key across tiers).
  - A running raid the move would put in the way of another raid at its gym stays at its tier, counted apart in the answer.
  - A screenshot whose known moment is more than 24 hours before its upload is not counted.
- **Staging (with the 8X stagings, Googer and Tester):** `/admin boss add pokemon:Sneasel tier:Dynamax 2★` while the feed lists it at 1★ and a Sneasel battle runs: the answer says the feed's 1★ is left out and "1 battle running was moved."; the post shows 2★ and "Staff moved **Dynamax Sneasel** to 2★."; `/max` offers only 2★; `/admin boss list` shows "moves it from Dynamax 1★"; `/admin boss remove` brings 1★ back and leaves the battle at 2★. Three Sneasel screenshots (two members or one, 2 heads) while it is listed at 1★: the moderators' channel gets the note once; a fourth says nothing more.

Screenshots there: a mention's attachments come without Message Content (passive only where it is on), so Pine can read them before deleting; the answer would go by DM (or a reply deleted after a minute) so nothing of a member's stays between the stops. The passive one making nothing is expected (the heads disagree with the list).

Step 13 passes (Tester's Rare Candy DM's Go to post opens the board's message). Step 22: `cutover-check` passes, no region line asks for Pin Messages; with every region boarded no boards line shows.

## From Phase 8M-8A (a party's popup is its post; prototype, 2026-10-26)

- **Question for Matt (review N5):** the design says a gym that shows a raid offers the raid, not "Report raid", so the map no longer offers "Report raid" under a gym's raids. Before 8M-8A it did, since an elite raid can be beside a standard one at the same gym. Should the map offer a way to report the second raid there (and how), or is `/raid` on Discord enough for that rare case? **Answered:** Matt's ruling of 2026-10-26 and plan 8E: one raid per gym, of any kind (an elite raid shows its egg on the gym, usually the evening before), so there is no second raid to report there, on the map or on Discord; the map offering none is right.
- **Built (closed 2026-10-26):** the plan's seven tasks (transcribed, each equal to its prototype commit; the brief went through an Opus builder check, the prototype an Opus review and a scoped re-review with two fix rounds), then the whole-branch review's six Minor fixes W-1 to W-6 (the places' pictures in a folder of their own, their keys looked up on a miss, a raid choice leaving a held form alone, a section's button drawn, the page forgetting ended parties' posts, the phone card named by its party).
- **Staging:** `2026-10-26-phase-8m8a-staging.md`, not yet run (with 8M-7's; needs the tunnel and a phone).
- **Next:** 8M-8B (the post's controls as web forms: Group… to choose or move, Meet…, +1s, the raid's changes under Discord's rule, staff's delete, Report to moderators, the completion question), then 8M-8C (Split…, Manage… beyond the conductor and kind, the drag-to-reorder route editor). Matt's icon notes ("From Phase 8M-7") start with 8M-8B's buttons and 8M-9's Report menu.

## From Phase 8R-9F (the region's board takes commands, closed 2026-10-27)

- **Built:** the board post "Today and coming up" open to members (unlocked once, at creation, take-over, the first draw of a board 8R-9 locked and `/admin setup-forums`; 6 hours of slowmode, exactly 21,600 s, is Pine's mark, so a moderator's later lock is left alone; no migration); `BoardKeeper` deletes what members write there (a member with Manage Messages kept, Q1), with a DM at most once a day per member (Q2), sweeps the boards at start and on reconnect (`bulkDelete`, the board opened and left open), checks the pin after any member's message, and is off in rehearsal mode; screenshots in the board downloaded, taken out and read by the channel's decisions, answered by one plain DM worded by deleted, kept or failed (R1); Manage Messages in the forum a `cutover-check` warning (R3); the stops' cap 3 → 10; Coming up names a kind once (M1, "Gigantamax Train · …"); a text-channel line that finds a deleted board waits for the remade board (M2, let go at the day's end and at shutdown). The prototype went through an Opus review (F1, F2 Important, seven Minor) and a re-review; the branch a whole-branch review (W1 the checklist with one member account, W2–W7 Minor), fixed as commits and re-reviewed.
- **Staging:** `2026-10-27-phase-8r9f-staging.md`, not yet run (no deploy needed; West's board at 5 s slowmode for the run, 6 h put back at the end). Step 19 notes that members can add reactions to the board's messages, which Pine can neither see nor remove (no reaction intent).
- **Reactions on Pine's posts (Matt, 2026-10-27):** best not reactable at all; failing that, Pine removes them; failing that, leaving them is no deal-breaker. Not reactable is possible: Add Reactions denied to everyone on the forum (as `reservePosts` denies Send Messages), and since Pine adds no reaction there, no member can join one either. But the board is a post in the region's forum, beside every raid, max battle, train and meetup post, and permissions are per forum, not per post: denying it there also stops reactions in every party post's chat. Matt (2026-10-27) wants members to keep reacting in party posts, so for the board post the second choice applies: Pine removes reactions from its own board messages wherever it reads them anyway (the sweep at start and on reconnect, and the message an edit of the summary or the stops returns; Manage Messages, which the board cleanup has), without the reaction intent. A reaction lasts until the next such read. Built as 8R-9G (below).
- **Minor, left open (X1):** the sweep at start asks for the pin check only when it deleted something; a board reopened while the bot was away by a moderator's kept message (or by messages already gone) stays unpinned until the summary's next edit or the stops' next draw. Fix: ask `changed(region.id)` whenever the sweep read a message that is neither the bot's own nor a system message.
- **Docs, left open (X2):** Discord's slowmode holds everyone but bots and members with Bypass Slowmode; Q1's moderators (Manage Messages) can write in the board once in six hours unless their role has Bypass Slowmode in the forum. Say so in the guides' board paragraph and the staging checklist (Googer, the owner, does not show it).

## From Phase 8E (an elite raid blocks its gym, closed 2026-10-27)

- **Built:** one raid per gym of any kind and per server (Matt, 2026-10-26: an elite raid shows its egg on the gym, usually the evening before), decided by `raidStandingAt` / `standsAtGym` (`src/domain/party/raid.ts`) for every report, `/location`, every raid change and `reportBarred`; a report of the other kind joins the raid there and changes nothing of it; 8X-J moves a raid only while it blocks its gym (a raid over by the clock is no longer brought back to Elite for a day); the refusal says "That gym already has a raid." The web stagings start with `npm run web:build`; `docs/web-setup.md` explains Cloudflare's Web Analytics beacon in the console. - **Question for Matt (provisional ruling 8E-1, in the code and `parties-and-posts.md`):** an Elite egg is reported with its hatch time on `/raid` and on the map (`reportedTime`, `ELITE_EGG_NEEDS_TIME`: "An Elite egg shows when it hatches: give its hatch time …"); without one it would stand its whole incubation (about a day) and, since 8E, keep every report off the gym. Still possible: a screenshot whose countdown or stage could not be read reports a timeless Elite egg, and a tier change (`/boss` making a timeless egg Elite, 8X-J's `moveBossTier`) leaves one. Do these need a rule too (refuse, or keep the standard incubation)?
- **Staging:** `2026-10-27-phase-8e-staging.md`, not yet run.
- **Method note:** the prototyper twice ran a stray empty `python -` heredoc (once a REPL that looped and wrote 64 MB before it was stopped by its own PID); the agent briefs forbid it, and it wrote nothing to the repository.
- **Next:** 8M-8C.

## From Phase 8R-9G (reactions off the board, closed 2026-10-27)

- **Built:** Pine removes the reactions on its own messages in the region's board (the summary and "Today’s Stops") after each edit of them (`clearReactions` on `editPost` / `editMessage`, only from the summary's and the stops' edits) and in `BoardKeeper`'s sweep at start and on reconnect (the bot's own messages, not Discord's system messages; an archived board reopened by a removal is checked for its pin); the discord.js cache forgets the removed reactions. A refusal never fails an edit and is logged at most twice per board (edit path and sweep, one wording). Party posts keep their reactions. Nothing is removed in rehearsal mode (a board is never a party's post; said once per channel). No reaction intent, no migration, no deploy. One prototype commit and one fix round (Opus review: R-F1 Important, the unpinned board after a removal; four Minor), re-reviewed ready; check 10,189 (1 skipped), build and database 611 green.
- **Staging:** step 19 of `2026-10-27-phase-8r9f-staging.md` (Tester reacts to the summary, a stops message and in a raid post's chat; the first two go at the next edit or a restart, the third stays).
- **Known limit:** an unchanged summary is not edited, so a reaction on it can stay until its text changes or the next start.
## From Phase 8M-8C (a train's Move…, Manage…, Split… and the route editor on the map, closed 2026-10-27)

- **Built:** the map's Move…, Manage… and Split… are Discord's dialogs as forms over the same functions (`moveTrainAs`, `manageTrainAs`, `splitGroupAs`; refusals `manageRefusal`, `splitRefusalFor`; the dialogs' builders `moveForm`, `manageForm`, `splitForm`), their refusals in Discord's words sent by the server (`MapTrain.moveRefusal`, `mayManage`); Move… is now Discord's Move… (Next, Previous, Skip), Finish, Reverse and the at-end mode only through Manage…, and the map's old presses `finish`, `reverse`, `at-end`, `kind` are gone (an old page's Move… does nothing until reloaded); `TrainService.reverse`, `setAtEnd`, `setKind` removed (tests go through `manage`). Route mode adds a Power Spot by a tap or Enter (gym and Power Spot dots are Tab stops only in route mode, `RouteFocus`), shows what lies ahead, and orders stops by dragging in the list (pointer and touch, a 32 px handle, autoscroll; a drag ends on Escape, a lost pointer, a redraw or an edit). `CONTROLS` has no "on Discord" left but starting a new group. The brief went through an Opus builder check (rulings R1–R9), the prototype an Opus review (2 Important, 9 Minor) and a scoped re-review (2 Minor), two fix rounds folded in; the branch a whole-branch review (B1–B5 fixed as commits, B6 kept) and a scoped re-review.
- **Ruled by Claude, for Matt to object to:** (1) the map's Move… lost Finish, Reverse and the end mode (they are in Manage…, as on Discord; R1, the anti-goal); (2) the conductor buttons stay outside Manage…, as `/conductor` on Discord (R2); (3) the route editor stays the side pane's route mode, docked under the map on a phone, not a form in the popup (R3); (4) Move… opens from the list even while the post controls' window is full, only its Submit is counted and refused; (5) Split… on the map has no slow-read limit (Discord's guards its dialogs only); (6) only Power Spots and gyms become Tab stops in route mode, not PokéStops (hundreds of them).
- **Staging:** `2026-10-27-phase-8m8c-staging.md` (step 0: `npm run web:build`, then start the bot), not yet run.
- **Method note:** Matt's new rule (2026-10-27) applied from fix round 1 on: fix rounds run the per-commit checks, the full suites once on the final prototype tip. The prototypers slipped three times into small shell forms the briefs forbid (an inline `node -e`, an empty heredoc, a `<<< ""`); nothing reached the repository.

Real-model `ocr:check` before the merge: no WORSE in any labelled folder, nothing late; Matt's 13 splits all cut by the divider, countdowns 23/23, kind 15/15; no single screen cut. Staging: `2026-10-27-phase-7p-staging.md`, not yet run.
- **Checking a worktree on real screenshots:** `ocr:check` decides no boss when the worktree's `var/game-data-cache.json` is of another `GAME_DATA_VERSION` ("The saved game data has another version"), and every boss then scores WORSE; copy a current cache in first (it happened here: the worktree's was version 7).
- **F1 (Minor, review):** a review record kept before 7P, or one the bot read whole because it ran late, whose picture `ocr:check` now cuts, scores against no pane (`memberLabelOfUnit`): it drops out of the baseline comparison instead of counting. Fix if it matters: score it against the first pane that shows a screen, as a `labels.json` label without `#n`.
- **Reading quality inside a pane, not mixing:** gyms and tier heads in small pillarboxed panes (Matt's 192 to 195), and on the full-height Duo fakes the max battle's countdown chip and raid gyms, are often misread. A plan for the reading of small panes, if real samples show it matters.
- **Unproven on real devices:** no real foldable or iPhone Duo screenshot yet; the fakes stand in (`var/ocr/fake-foldable-2026-10-26/`, 103: Galaxy Fold, Pixel Fold (wide, panes from Matt's uneven splits), Pixel 9/10 Pro Fold (near square), the iPhone Duo, single screens of those shapes, GO beside drawn chat and notes panes, Matt's divider splits with one pane a dark or black chat). Foldable support is claimed only after a real sample. The same for GO beside another app. Matt (2026-10-27): his phone's landscape split is fixed at 50:50, its portrait split can be moved; GO beside another app is likely the most common split. Timings measured on the loaded laptop are upper bounds (a split refused late there read in time alone).
- **Ruled by Claude, for Matt to confirm:** the panes' time budget is 85% of `ocr.timeoutSeconds`, predicted once from the whole reading's time with a floor of 0.8; a split whose panes would not fit is refused `two-screens` (logged `late`) rather than reported mixed. A seam cut (GO beside a map or an app) is tried on pictures under 1.6 times as tall as wide. In the board a split refused `two-screens` says "Send each screen on its own."
- **Known limits:** a top/bottom split without a divider where one pane is the map (`cut-1000038180`) keeps today's whole reading; 180's clock reads a map chip (6:38), as before 7P; a max battle's two-part chip is no countdown (out of scope). Review S4: a chat app below Android's divider (dark #313338 or black) is not cut in 4 of 6 fakes (the divider's bordering rows are not picture-like): the whole reading stands, either the raid reported right or refused unclear, the chat's text never in a report; above the divider it is cut in all 6. A map pane beside a raid on a foldable can lend a map chip's time as the clock (the fakes' map crop has no status bar of its own).

## From Phase 8B-L (2026-10-27)

Finding B1 fixed: the raid boss list is read from Leek Duck's raid manifest (`raids/manifest.json`, `settings.raidBosses.manifestUrl` with a default) and the page of each rotation in force (`src/domain/pogo/raid-manifest.ts`, `src/services/raid-rotations.ts`), read again 5 s after the next local rotation change; ScrapedDuck's `raids.json` stays the fallback. Prototype 869b0619 and 9e992044, review fixes 2bc01a24 (R1–R9) and 77dc33d9 (V1, V2), rebased as 72595dcf, 13dc6230, 99273385, 5a5ced4d. Full check 10,390 passed (1 skipped), build, database 611. Staging checklist `2026-10-27-phase-8bl-staging.md`, not yet run (no deploy, no migration; a restart takes it).

- **Decided by the controller (object if wrong):** a gap between rotations (Halloween Part I ends 20:00, the Giratina week starts 06:00) keeps the rotation that ended last, per kind (regular, shadow), until the next starts, at most 24 h (R1). A timeout, no answer or 5xx of Leek Duck while the list is the manifest's is a failed sync with the quick retries; the fallback is read for structural causes (404, not JSON, no rotation, no boss) or once the retries are spent (R2). With the manifest the first read of a run is at once.
- **Decided by the prototyper, agreed in review:** rotations at a city's own times (`local_time: false`) are left out by the one rule of 8R-7 (`isForEveryone`), Leek Duck's "Exclusive" tag not read. Overlapping rotations are merged by tier heading, the one that started last giving its headings (a Raid Day inside a week: its heading is the Raid Day's for its hours, the week's other tiers stay; Leek Duck's page shows only one rotation). No shadow rotation in force is no shadow bosses. A shadow rotation's headings are read as `Shadow <heading>`.
- **To watch in staging (V3):** the gap rule needs Leek Duck to keep listing the ended rotation overnight (the sample listed the Palkia week 7 h 40 min after its end). If it drops it, the read falls back for the night; the checklist's step "Over a gap" watches for it.

## Nearby stops first on Discord (Matt, 2026-10-27/28; no plan of its own, commits 1f76a6db..9ed149c7 on web-polish)

- **Built:** places near those already named come first in the stop lists. Typed text: distance to the anchors breaks ties after fit, the member's region, the kind and extra words (`mergePlaces`, `nearerFirst`; `PlaceFinder.find(…, { kindsFirst, anchors })`); no anchors, today's order. An empty field (Matt: "Yes, nearest first"): `/research report`'s `stop2:`…`stop5:` list the PokéStops within 2 km of the stops already filled, those named left out; `/route add stop:` lists the gyms and Power Spots nearest the route's last stop (a gone one: the stop before), no radius, the route's stops left out, `kindsFirst` not reordering; `/rocket report stop:` (Matt: "can do that but not crucial") lists the PokéStops within 2 km of the member's own last report today (Matt: "Last today"), the stop itself first. One pure `src/domain/search/nearest.ts` (`anchorMetres`, `nearerFirst`, `nearestPlaces`), used by `PlaceFinder.nearest` (choices built only for the places kept) and by the web map's `nearestLines`. Labels of the empty lists end in the distance (`distanceWords`) when they fit uncut. The last report is a record per member in `StopReportService` (a `BoundedMap` of 5,000; written on every saved report, research or Rocket, Discord or map; another member's replace or refine leaves it; the member's own Take back of it clears it; after a restart it falls back to the table), read within 1 second (else no anchor) and not for a pasted link. Also fixed: an ended research row of the current period is no "replaced" report (R1). No command shape changed: no deploy needed. Reviewed four times (Opus), all findings fixed.
- **Claude's calls, for Matt to object to:** (1) staff taking back a member's latest report keeps that member's record (only one's own Take back clears it); (2) a hideout put back over a grunt posted nowhere keeps the grunt reporter's record; (3) typed lists keep places already named (only the empty lists leave them out); (4) a long name drops the distance from its label rather than being cut more (Matt: either is fine, the order honors distance).
- **Matt's answer (2026-10-28):** (1) fine.
- **Staging:** none written; try `/research report` with `stop:` set then an empty `stop2:`, `/route add` on a route with stops, and `/rocket report` after a report, on the dev bot.

## From the staging run of the web polish (8M-7 to 8M-10, 8E, nearby stops first; 2026-10-28)

Main checkout at typescript-migration d5392492, `var/stage/settings.json`, logs `bot-40` to `bot-42` (bot-41 with `distanceUnits: 'metric'` and no moderators' channel, for 8M-7 23 and 8M-8B 22; the settings put back after). Googer in the app's browser pane driven by Claude; Tester on Matt's phone and, in the afternoon, in Claude in Chrome (Matt logged it in). Every result, with the words seen, is in the git-ignored `var/stage/results/web-polish-2026-10-28.md`.

- **Passes:** almost every step that two accounts can run, on the desktop (light and dark, 1024 to 1280) and on Matt's phone: 8M-7 (the frame, the list and its sorts, layers, the phone's sheet, imperial and metric), 8M-8A (the popup is the post, for raids, eggs, max battles, trains and meetups; no member ids in the JSON; no Discord or Google picture), 8M-8B (+1/−1, the completion question both ways, Group… in every case two accounts make, Meet… and its window, Delete…, Report… with and without the moderators' channel and its window, the 30-presses window shared with Discord), 8E (an elite raid and a standard raid block each other's gym on `/raid` and the map, the Elite egg's hatch time required, `/location` and `/boss` honor it), 8M-8C (Move…, Manage…, Split…, the route editor with mouse, keyboard and a finger; refusals in Discord's words), 8M-9A (the Report button and its steps, max battles drawn and reported, Power Spot popups, Gyms off by default), 8M-9B (the Research and Rocket layers, popups, Take back, Filter…, both forms, Add stops… and Same task at more stops… from the last stop, the contradiction question), 8M-10 (who is offered the editor, on and off, a form holds it, the look, "No overlaps or gaps.").
- **Fails:** S11 (below), and 8M-8C 28a on a phone (S25).
- **Not run:** what needs a third account (8M-8C 22, 8E 5, 8M-8B 13's several-groups half, 8M-8B 21b), Discord cut off from the bot (8M-8B 5, 8M-8C 9's read-only half, 8M-9A 9's, 8M-10 9), a permission change (8M-8B 21, 21d), screenshots at hand (8E 5, 6, 8M-8A 7), the dev database by hand (8M-8A 11), Geoman drags and Fix… on a gap and a save (8M-10 12 to 15), a second staff account (8M-10 8b), the OpenStreetMap fallback (8M-7 11: Stadia served the tunnel's address too), `/gym review` (none pending), `/admin boss hide` (8M-9A 13), and the time-passed half of 8M-8B 17. Matt has no third account: those steps stay covered by the unit tests only.
- **Checklist fixes:** 8M-8C 9: with a conductor, everybody else gets the conductor's sentence, signed up or not ("You are not signed up for this train." only without one, `mayNotMove`); 8M-8B 6: +1 from not signed up signs up with none more; 8M-8A 18 and 19: no "Do this on Discord" for −1/+1/Meet…, Move… is 8M-8C's dialog; 8M-9A 17: the menu also has Field research and Team GO Rocket; 8M-9B 11: a plain stop opens as a popup on the phone (edge on the left), not a docked card: check which the design means.

**Findings for a fix pass (8M-11):**

- **S1 (Important):** the popup's post lags one change behind a press (up to one 20 s poll): the post comes from `layoutOf`, the layout last shown on Discord, while the press's edit is still in flight. Fix: the press answers once its edit is drawn (bounded), or the page asks for the post again when it lands. Matt: the live updating is otherwise better than he expected.
- **S9, S20, S21 (Matt):** answers that land as a line under the buttons are missed: "Start a new group on Discord →", refusals ("Only the conductor…"), and the completion question inside an open card. A note box in place of the form with the link as a button, and a shake of the card or popup for a refusal. Meet…'s "Your group changed meanwhile: try again." keeps the form open and Submit says it again: say "press Meet… again", or close it as Group… does (13b); check Discord's sentence too (one path).
- **S11 (Minor, confirmed):** when a focused list line's party ends, the focus falls to the page, not the sort: `list-ui.ts` looks for `select[data-sort]` inside the redrawn part, and the sort lies outside it.
- **S2:** a meetup's ring at a raid's gym covers the raid's time badge. **S3:** a popup's × is small on a phone. **S4:** several things at one place are headed "Here": the place's name. **S5:** the badge in lower case ("hatched"): "Hatched". **S6:** "in 24 hours" where Discord says "in a day". **S16:** 8M-4's Change… ignores Escape. **S17:** research answers in the notice band run together with " - ": lines. **S18:** Add stops… and Same task at more stops… offer stops that already hold the task. **S19, S28:** "30m left" vs "goes away in 29 min", and in metric "17m left" beside "410 m": minutes as "17 min", rounded alike. **S22 (Matt):** "What's on" → "What's going on". **S25 (Minor, Matt: an edge case):** a finger on the map while another holds a stop's handle in the docked pane pinch-zooms: Leaflet counts every touch on the screen; switch the map's touch zoom and drag off while a stop is held. **S26 (Matt):** the research form's task can be changed after it is picked, without Cancel. **S24 (Matt):** dragging a stop shows a line between the two stops it will land between.
- **S13 (Matt):** the gyms' clusters are choppy zoomed out and too many: profile first, then a larger radius at low zooms.
- **S14 (Matt):** the Report menu's icons: the Dynamax icon from the game by hand (from the official map's bundle, follow-ups "From Phase 8M-9A"); the game has no train, so Claude draws a train icon in the style of the game's icons, two or three for Matt to choose from, then also the application emoji on Discord if he likes it.
- **S27 (Matt):** a train's line shows its direction (arrowheads, flipped when a back-and-forth train turns; the part done fainter) and a looping train its closing leg, dashed.
- **S29 to S32 (Matt, the region editor):** S29 the editor needs a mouse or trackpad as well as 1024 px (`(pointer: fine)`), the refusal naming both; S30 layer toggles for gyms, PokéStops and Power Spots in the editor, clustered zoomed out; S31 no warning at Back to the map, but "Edit regions · N unsaved" in the menu and a line in the editor (Matt agreed); S32 with "Move shared corners together" on, deleting a shared corner deletes it in every region of this server that shares it, one undo step, refused if any region would keep fewer than three corners (Matt: yes).
- **The 8M-11 design** (`docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-28-web-fix-pass-design.md`, 2026-10-28) takes all of the above, with Matt's icon choices: train C3b (Pine's own), the game's Dynamax icon (kept as `pictures/icons/dynamax.png`), the game's calendar for meetups, Campfire's Poké Ball for spawns.
- **Observations:** the notice band keeps the last answer and pushes the map's controls down; the editor's first opening took 30 to 40 s on the loaded laptop (timing not trusted); the Report → Max battle step listed four spots, not five; "meetup safety is off" is logged twice at start.

**Ideas and questions, not for 8M-11 unless Matt says so:** S7 (a train selectable at every stop ahead: numbered dots in its color), S10 (a Light / Dark / Device choice), S12 (max battles in the Raids layer or their own; Claude leans to Raids, Matt undecided), S23 (Matt, agreed as Claude sketched it, after the fix pass: "Find a place or report": gyms, PokéStops and Power Spots by name (a server search for stops and spots, which are now only read per view) and what is reported now, raids, battles, trains, meetups, research by task or reward and Rocket, filtered in the page; a pick flies there and opens its popup).

- **Left on the test server:** the raids at Locks on Schenley Bridge (with Tester's test report to the moderators in its post), Dippy, Schenley Fountain and Cathy end by themselves; Tester's research at Roboceptionist replaced Googer's; five test reports in #meetup-review; "Staging popup train" with six stops, Googer conducting.
- **Next:** the fix pass 8M-11 (design first, with the train icon mockups), then the design of Find (S23).

## Matt's rulings of 2026-10-29 (the open calls of every plan, one by one)

The 67 open rulings, calls and defaults left "for Matt" in the sections above were gone through with Matt. Every one is kept, except those below; a "for Matt" line above is settled by this section.

- 8E-1: refuse a timeless Elite egg from a screenshot or a tier change (ruling 1).
- 8X-S: ask when a cut Power Spot name fits spots in other regions (ruling 18).
- 8W-3: no #bot-updates post for a stop-only import (ruling 50).
- 2E-2: a line in the admin guide: keeping a member out of a forum needs a permission overwrite (ruling 45).
- Max bosses: catch CP ranges in their posts (ruling 67).
- Web: T1, A1, A2, I1, L1, L2, O1, S7, S10 (staging of 2026-10-29) and the checklist fixes (8M-11A 5, 8M-11C 25 and 27).
- 8W-P: scrub the private tool's docs before its first push (ruling 40).

- **Also kept with a reason Matt gave:** `/rank` at level 50 (the level 40 cap is effectively retired); `/counters` attackers at level 40 by default (level 40 gets about 90% of the way at much lower cost); Remote on meetups (only because it costs nothing); a spawn's reporter always named (as legacy Pine); max battles stay in the Raids layer (a separate toggle if members ask); the rehearsal rules revisited at the rehearsal.
- **Still parked:** an unlisted gym as a pin, Pokebattler as a fallback boss source, spot targets, more than five groups, the ending-soon question for `/raid`.

- **Matt's decisions on the map's popups (2026-10-29), for the small web plan before the cutover:**
  - **The list of things at one place:** headed by the name of the tapped place; under it the things at that place (the gym itself, its raid, a train stopping there), then a small label "Also nearby" over the things only near it on the screen; "Nearby" as the heading when none is at a named place. Matt: useful on a phone, where a near neighbor is easily tapped by mistake. If the grouping does not work out, the heading "At this spot" instead of "Here".

- **Matt's decision on a train's stop badges (2026-10-29), for the small web plan before the cutover:** the numbered badges set off left of each stop (8M-13 task 2, S7) look out of place, worst zoomed out (a fixed pixel offset). Instead:
  - Each badge is a small number on the route line itself, centered at the stop, with the gym's or Power Spot's marker drawn above it (its tap wins).
  - It counts the stops ahead from the train's current stop (1 = next) instead of the place in the route.
  - The train's popup says "next" or "in N stops" beside the stops ahead, so that the map's numbers and the route's (`/route`, "Whole route") meet.
  - The badges still show from zoom 15 only.
- **Matt (2026-10-30): a route may list a stop twice.** Legacy Pine refused it (`route-add.js`, "is already a part of this route"; conductors moved the stop after its first visit instead), and so does Pine today (`addStop`, `already-on-route`). Matt then (same day): a minor point, likely not needed, since a train that redoes its gyms now loops or turns back, both covered; parked, not planned. Should it come back, it is a design of its own: what finds a stop by its key rather than its place in the route (the map's route-mode tap that adds or takes off a stop, Move… to a named stop, `/route remove`, saved routes, the editor's drag list, `hasStop`). 8M-15's badges already show two numbers for such a stop.

## From the staging run of 8M-16 (2026-10-31)

Googer alone, in the built-in browser with a second map tab where one was needed (Claude in Chrome never connected, so no Tester); `bot-76.log` to `bot-78.log`, the last two at debug level; the named tunnel.

- **Passed:** step 1 (a raid: signal at once, list within 1 s, a second signal merged at the 5 s window); 2 (a press in an open popup shown in 1.6 s, not one behind); 3 (about 0.6 s to the signal, the post about 1 s after); 4 (grunt, take back, research: each within 1 s); 5 (a hatch on the map within 2 s of the moment); 7 on a desktop (a form keeps its typed value and focus; route mode's panel the same element at the same scroll through a change; a tall popup's scroll, after the finding below); 9 (hidden, by faking visibility: the pane keeps background tabs visible); 10 (tunnel down a couple of minutes: 530s from Cloudflare, polling at 20 s, no burst, the stream back by itself, then quiet); 11 (bot restarted: 502s, live again in under a minute).
- **Step 13, in part:** streams ended by renewal at 30.5, 32.0, 33.4, 34.1, 34.5 and 34.6 minutes, and a tab kept the 2-minute pace for over three hours. A change was not timed on a stream that old.
- **Finding, fixed twice:** a tall popup went back to its top at every redraw. 6db09521 restored it after the reopening, and 1158d92d after each `popup.update()`: Leaflet lets the height go to measure, and the post of the change arrives after the list, so the first fix alone did nothing live (its unit test passed, the fake did not do this). Seen live after the second: 100 before, 100 through the list and the post. A popup stops scrolling at all when the window has grown since it was made, since `maxHeight` is taken when it is drawn: not a fault.
- **Numbers for step 15, from one or two maps, so they decide nothing yet:** `/api/parties` median 5 ms, p90 322 ms, most 361 ms (the high ones are cold member look-ups after a restart); `/api/stop-reports` median 6 ms, most 172 ms; a post's ask 118 ms; `waiting` never above 1. The member route's round trip and `X-RateLimit-Limit` are not in the log: a staging at load would need a line for them. The soft lifetime and the spread's 20 a second stay as they are.
- **Not run:** 6 (needs Tester; a real-redraw test covers it); 8 and the phone card's scroll with a tall post (Matt's phone; on an emulated phone the card stays open and updates through a change, too short to scroll); 12 (a quick tunnel, Matt starts it); 14 (30 streams: 4 per member and 8 per address need several members or a scratch cap). For the rehearsal or a later staging.

Matt would rather see the minutes (2026-10-31), so the map says minutes below 90 ("Hatches in 55 minutes, at 7:31 PM"); Discord keeps its own words, so the two differ from 45 to 89 minutes. Not a fault: taking back a research report that had replaced another leaves the stop with none, since a replacement deletes the row it replaces (`stop-reports.md`).

## From /counters for megas newer than the game data (2026-10-31)

Matt ran `/counters` in raid posts during the Mega Chandelure event (14:23 and 15:46, `bot-76.log`) and was refused although Pokebattler's site has the boss: Pokebattler answers `CHANDELURE_MEGA`, and PokeMiners' game master has no mega for Chandelure, so the boss list and the raid hold it by name. `unlistedMega` now reads such a name.

- **Claude's calls, for Matt to object to:** a typed `Mega <species>` that is not on the boss list asks for a `tier`, as any boss does, and with a tier Pokebattler is asked (an unreal mega gets "does not know"). Only the average of the boss's moves is offered, since its moves are not in the game data. The boss list's name-held megas are offered first in `boss`'s list. `/counters` and the posts' catch CP read these names; DMs and the map's own data still say only the name. A typed `Mega Pikachu`, a mega that does not exist, is asked for a tier rather than refused (the reviewer's Minor 2, kept: Pine cannot tell it from a mega the feed will name next).
- **Not proven:** the words Matt saw are not in the log; the refusal is inferred from the two quick uses without a Pokebattler line.
- **Staged in part (2026-10-31, `bot-78.log`):** `/counters boss:Mega Chandelure tier:Mega` on the test server asked the real Pokebattler and answered in 4.5 s (Hydreigon first), without a tier it asks for one, and "Change options…" opens with only the average under Boss moves.
- **The rest, at the next mega the game master lacks, or with `/admin boss add` if one can be held by name:** 1. `/counters` in the raid's post answers with the feed's picture and the boss's name. 2. `/counters boss:Mega <species>` elsewhere takes the tier of the list, and the name is offered while typing. 3. "Change options…" opens with only the average under Boss moves, and a submit answers. 4. Once the game master has the mega, the same post still answers (by the catalogue's entry, with its moves).

## From the small security fixes (2026-11-01)

- **The overrides** of `glob` and `minimatch` in `package.json` (advisories of 2026-10-30) go once `@fastify/static`, eslint, typescript-eslint and testcontainers' `archiver` ask for fixed versions themselves.
- **Seen at the dev stack's update of 2026-11-01:** the bot, the watchdog and the tunnel run under their new limits, healthy and connected. A screenshot report is still to show that the reader works under them.
- **A regression that update found:** `redirect: 'error'` on the feeds (7e021457) stopped the raid boss sync, since Leek Duck answers a rotation's `<slug>.html` with a 308 to `<slug>`; the check before it covered the manifest and not its pages. The feeds now follow a redirect on their own host only (`fetchOnItsHost`), checked against every real address with all the rotation pages.
- **Backups already made** keep mode 644 until they are pruned: `chmod 600` them by hand, or let them age out.
- **Public Bot** is off on both applications (Matt, 2026-11-01); `cutover-check` warns if it comes back on.

## From Phase 8Z (the security pass before the cutover, 2026-11-01)

Nine items, a commit each, branch `security-pass`; its staging checklist is `2026-11-01-phase-8z-staging.md`, not yet run. No migration and no command definition changes. **On deploy:** set `googleByHandPerMonth` low on the dev stack beside its low budget; `./pine update` brings the service `docker-proxy` and the volume `watchdog-state`.

- **Claude's calls, for Matt to object to:**
  - **Meetups.** The refusal for anybody else is "Only its maker and moderators can change this meetup's name and information."; a blocked maker's "You are blocked from meetups on this server, so you can't change this one.", with Appeal… on the command, in words pointing to `/meetup` at the Submit and on the map. `/name` reads the block within a dialog's time as `/info` does, one guard for both. The notice to the moderators: who, the text as it is now, Go to post and Go to request; a heading of its own naming the meetup; staff's own changes too; never old and new side by side. A change that changed nothing tells nobody.
  - **Pins.** `/wild`'s and the map's own sentences (`WILD_PIN_BLOCKED`, `MAP_SPAWN_BLOCKED`, 403 on the map, as `/max`'s there).
  - **Group pings.** Remembered in memory for the last 10,000 parties.
  - **Floods.** One sentence for the cap on every path (`OPEN_PARTIES_FULL`), which tells of waiting meetups. The map asks the cap before its limits, and the split's Submit asks it before `/train`'s window; on Discord `submitSplit` runs after the router counted the press, so a refused Submit there has used one press of 30 a minute. A split's use goes back within what `Throttles.giveBack` allows, twice the limit in a window, so four Submits in 15 minutes that all fail (the forum refusing posts) then hear "You can use /train again in …": the same as `/train`. The window's refusal on the button and `/group split` names `/train`, so that the member knows what counted.
  - **Map and login load.** 60 a minute counts whatever goes through `actingOnTheMap`, a form's opening too, since each may force a fetch; its words are "That was a lot of changes in a minute. Try again in a moment." A code is `^[A-Za-z0-9._~-]{1,128}$`. A 429's `Retry-After` is honored; the login fails for that while with `?login=failed`.
  - **Google by hand.** A lookup counts when either of its two questions reached Google. The `/gym refresh` answer is one sentence with when the month starts again. The cap's count also shows in `/gym google`, as a line of its own.
  - **Delete….** One sentence for a member and for a moderator without the permission: "Only admins and moderators with the permission Manage Messages can do that."
  - **The proxy.** `wollomatic/socket-proxy:1`, a moving tag as the file's other images have, fetched anew by `./pine update`, with a line in `./pine`. It runs as user 0 without capabilities on a read-only file system: its own example gives the socket's group, whose id differs per host. Its limits are 64 MB and 64 processes. `SP_ALLOW_GET` and `SP_ALLOW_POST` as the review wrote them. The watchdog's `DOCKER_SOCKET` became `DOCKER_PROXY` (`host:port`), `SocketDockerApi` became `EngineDockerApi`, `containerIds` became `containerNames`.
- **From the whole-branch review:** the watchdog tells the owner when it cannot reach Docker for `WATCHDOG_ALERT_MINUTES` (`docker-away`, once per spell), and the proxy has its image's health check, seen healthy on the laptop's engine. Claude's call: `mayOpenParty` keeps only the rule of the cap, and `openPartiesFull` says who is held to it, since it is the one caller. `/train`'s guard reads the cap before its deferral without `readInTime`: it opens no dialog.
- **Checked on the laptop's engine, 2026-11-01** (compose.yaml's own service, scratch containers, no bot, no secrets, all removed afterwards): the list, and this stack's bot by name, pass, with or without an API version; `create`, `archive`, `export`, `logs`, `top`, `exec`, `stop`, `kill`, another container, a one-off's name, and the bot by its hex id are refused with 403, `PUT` and `DELETE` with 405; the proxy cannot be reached from outside its network; a restart of a container that ignores SIGTERM took 152 seconds through it and answered 204; the image has linux/arm64; a new volume takes a folder's `node` owner, and a read-only `ops` volume reads, with a scratch image.
- **Unproven until the checklist runs:** nothing of this has met Discord; the proxy on the Air's OrbStack (its arm64 image running, the socket's owner there), the bot's real image under the watchdog's user, and a real restart by the watchdog through the proxy.
- **Not done here:** the status in `CLAUDE.md` and the memory are the controller's at the plan's close, and so is the Gantt chart's review row (`npm run gantt -- review 8Z …`). The chart's task dates are the times the commits were first made: a folding of wording afterwards moved their shas, not the work.

## From inactive stops as Power Spots (2026-11-02)

`withGameKinds` (`packages/wayspot-core/src/export-file.ts`), applied by `/gym import`: a stop Wayfarer has as `INACTIVE` is taken for a Power Spot, in older files too. No change to the export tool or the file's format. Checked on one Wayspot in the game (a Power Spot, no PokéStop on the game's map); the rest rest on the rule. Inactive Power Spots stay Power Spots: they come and go from day to day (Matt), so a Power Spot missing from the game's map says nothing.

Matt on the sources: with this rule Wayfarer's map is reliable for Power Spots. What it misses are sponsored locations, and those are only ever stops or gyms as far as he has seen, so a sponsored list needs no Power Spots.
